## M-111 Destructive-command doctrine — pure layer DONE (2026-11-07 keeper tick)
- Existing basis found (composition, not duplication): the tree already refuses a denylist of destructive shell invocations outright on the execution road (mythic_agent/core/sandbox.py — _DENYLIST_PATTERNS: rm -rf, mkfs, dd of=/dev/..., shred / wipefs / blkdiscard, shutdown / reboot, fork bomb, raw block-device overwrite, judged by Sandbox.check_denied), already answers command permission with deny / ask / allow in its postures (mythic_agent/core/permission_profiles.py, M-095 — COMMAND_ANSWERS deny / ask / allow, Trusted Forge's command answer is ask), already asks afresh through the ask mode of the elder ToolPolicy (mythic_agent/core/policy.py — modes read-only / ask / trusted), and already knows a remembered approval is only ever created with the caller's explicit answer (mythic_agent/core/remembered_approvals.py, M-096 — explicit=True). Nothing in the tree held the ladder itself as data — which acts are reversible, which are costly, which can never be undone, and what approval each kind needs — and no road named an irreversible act's targets in plain words before judging it. Built in team-07 on banked base 00268f5 = M-110 closure (110/272), M-111 was built as that missing doctrine layer ON those roads, not replacing any of them: its pattern shapes widen the shipped sandbox denylist's shapes (this layer classifies where that road refuses), its ask-once approval IS the tree's ask answer, and its explicit approval is the M-096 explicit-answer rule with the roadmap's plain-words rule added. Same sibling shape as M-110/M-108: frozen validated dataclasses, build_/validate_ pairs with alternate spellings, PLAIN_EXPLANATION, USAGE, shared parser and formatters, deterministic-id registry, and a *_record function shaped as session news.
- Command-surface name chosen: /doctrine — the slice's own subject word, in the one-word lowercase house style of /audit, /halt, /secrets, /budgets; checked first: no /doctrine surface and no doctrine command existed in the tree (the only doctrine mentions are prose in method_charter.py), and no destructive_doctrine module name clashed.
- Built: mythic_agent/core/destructive_doctrine.py — DESTRUCTIVE_DOCTRINE_SLICE = "M-111" + DOCTRINE_RUNGS (reversible / costly / irreversible — exactly the roadmap's three words in roadmap order; NO extra safe base rung was added, because no sibling doctrine carries one and an unplaced command is handled honestly as unclassified instead) + APPROVAL_REQUIREMENTS (none / ask_once / explicit_named_targets — none for reversible, ask_once for costly grounded in the permission-profile ask answer and the ToolPolicy ask mode, explicit_named_targets for irreversible grounded in M-096's explicit rule) + APPROVAL_PHRASES saying each approval in plain words + DoctrineRung frozen dataclass (also exported as Rung: the approval is derived from the rung when unnamed and a rung built naming any other approval refuses, so a rung never promises less than the doctrine asks) + DOCTRINE_LADDER held as validated data + rung_for / approval_for_rung / validate_rung / build_rung + DestructivePattern frozen dataclass (pattern_id via normalize_pattern_id, rung via normalize_rung, label as clean text, regex validated by compiling it, an anchor word targets are read after, and an implicit_target phrase held as data for acts whose command names no target) + validate_pattern / build_pattern / pattern_for_id + COMMAND_PATTERNS, the 29 held shapes as validated data in the judge's precedence order (the dry_run reading first — a command declaring --dry-run, or git clean / push -n, changes nothing; then the irreversible shapes; then the costly shapes; then the reversible readings — so an act matching two rungs earns the worse one) + judge_command, the mechanical judge (a command text, or its argv words as data; patterns read in held order and the first match places the act; a command no pattern matches is judged unclassified — no rung, no pattern, approval ask_once — and is never claimed safe; an empty command refuses) + DestructiveJudgement frozen dataclass (also exported as Judgement: command + argv as data; a classified judgement must name a held pattern of its own rung and carries that rung's one approval; an unclassified judgement names no rung and no pattern; the classification, pattern and approval are validated against each other on construction so a judgement can never disagree with itself; a judgement on the irreversible rung REFUSES to exist without at least one named target) + plain_words, the roadmap's rule as code (for an irreversible act the sentence names every target and says it cannot be undone before anything else; costly names its targets and its price; unclassified states plainly it is not claimed safe) + doctrine_record returning the session-news dict (type destructive_act, news true, judgement id, command, argv, rung, classification, pattern, approval, targets, and the plain-words headline) for EVERY judged act — a judged destructive act is always news, never lost as a log line (the M-108 halt_record pattern) + DoctrineRegistry, an in-memory storage-neutral registry model (register / judge judging and holding in one move / get / list_judgements in the order judged / record announcing one held judgement; caller-supplied ids used as given with duplicates refused and unknown ids raising KeyError, unsupplied ids the deterministic sequence judgement-1, judgement-2, ... skipping numbers a supplied id already took — no randomness a test cannot pin) + PLAIN_EXPLANATION + USAGE and the shared parse_doctrine_args for the /doctrine surface (empty/show -> ("show", None); ladder -> ("ladder", None); show <judgement> or a bare judgement id -> ("show", judgement); check <command...> -> ("check", command) with everything after check taken as the command however many words it takes; a grammar word in the wrong shape — a bare rung word, a bare pattern id, check with no command — is a USAGE error, never a judgement id) and formatters (format_rung / format_ladder stating every rung's approval in words, format_pattern / format_patterns naming every held shape with its rung, format_judgement with rung and approval always stated and an unclassified judgement stated as not classified and not claimed safe, format_judgements with the plain "no commands judged" empty statement, format_doctrine_record / format_record, format_doctrine_screen the whole slice — status stated in words wherever shown, never colour or symbol alone). The classifier's shapes, stated plainly: irreversible — rm with recursive+force in any flag spelling (short combined, split, or long), mkfs / mke2fs / mkswap, shred / wipefs / blkdiscard, dd of=/dev/..., redirection onto a raw block device, DROP TABLE / DATABASE / SCHEMA and TRUNCATE TABLE in a command, git push with --force / --force-with-lease / -f, git reset --hard, git clean with -f, and the fork bomb; costly — any other rm, redirection overwriting a file (a /dev/null target excluded), git push, git reset, git checkout -- / git restore discards, git branch -d / -D, mv, recursive chmod / chown, kill / killall / pkill, piping into a shell, shutdown / reboot / poweroff, truncate, and find -delete; reversible — the dry-run reading, read-only programs (ls / cat / grep / echo and kin with no destructive shape), git status / log / diff / show and kin, branch switching, and mkdir / touch. Targets are read mechanically per shape: a redirection names its file, a dd names its of= device, a drop names its database object, other shapes name the operands after their anchor word, and an irreversible act naming no operand takes its pattern's held implicit_target phrase (a bare force push names "the current branch on its remote", a bare hard reset names "uncommitted work in the working tree", a forced clean names "untracked files in the working tree", a fork bomb names "every process on this machine") — named from held data, never guessed at judge time. The module holds the ladder, the patterns and the judgements as data only: it runs no command, deletes nothing, writes no file, changes no setting, and imports no agent / UI code (stdlib re / shlex only).
- Verification on the laptop (PYTHONPATH=$PWD, repo .venv-hammer): tests/test_destructive_doctrine.py 37 passed (pure only — ladder data validation and the derived-approval refusal, pattern data validation, the rm -rf class in every flag spelling naming its targets including inside a compound command, disk / device / SQL-drop irreversibles, force push and hard reset with implicit targets, dry-run reversibility, costly push / reset / discard / overwrite-redirect naming its file, reversible readings needing no approval, unknown-command honesty (unclassified, ask_once, never claimed safe), argv-as-data judging, judgement self-consistency refusals including an irreversible judgement refusing without named targets, the news record, registry deterministic ids with skip-and-duplicate behaviour, parser grammar and refusals, and formatters). Neighbours: tests/test_kill_switch.py 59 + tests/test_secret_hygiene.py 51 + tests/test_budgets.py 67 + tests/test_arch_conformance.py 11 = 188 passed; five-file combined run 225 passed. One test-side mistake of mine was caught and fixed during the run (a build_rung call written with a bare string where the sibling build shape takes a mapping — the module's refusal was correct); no defects found in the new module or in any sibling layer.

PUBLIC_API inventory (+ the destructive_doctrine names in destructive_doctrine.__all__) + OWNERSHIP regen, roadmap flip M-111 to done (111/272 at banking), ledger row; dead-code quarantine entries if the banking gate flags the new surface (M-074 pattern).

## M-114 Remote and SSH work — pure layer DONE (2026-11-07 forge worker)
- Existing basis found (composition, not duplication): a workspace already has a portable identity and contained file resolution (mythic_agent/core/workspace.py — canonical_workspace_root / workspace_id / resolve_file: one directory on THIS machine, one identity), but nothing described a project living on ANOTHER machine. Background jobs already hold a command as data (mythic_agent/core/background_tasks.py, M-103 — an argv list of words, never a shell string, because a single string is the shape a shell would eval), but nothing held such a command as work meant for a remote machine, through a named connection, against a named remote workspace root. Multi-repo workspaces hold several repos under one session (mythic_agent/core/multi_repo.py, M-113), but every root they name is a root the session itself can reach. No shipped ssh/remote/transport road existed to compose with (grep over mythic_agent/core: the only ssh mentions are secret-hygiene path rules, and the only remote mentions are commit_craft.py git-remote banking words — neither is a remote-work road), so M-114 was built as that missing remote layer in team-10 on banked base c0e04ff = M-113 closure (113/272), in the exact sibling shape of M-113/M-112: frozen validated dataclasses with alias exports, build_/validate_ pairs with alternate spellings, closed vocabularies with normalize functions, PLAIN_EXPLANATION, USAGE, shared parser and formatters, deterministic-id registry, and a *_record function shaped as session news. The one shipped discipline composed with directly is M-103 argv-as-data: a remote command IS an argv word list validated by the same rule, and a shell string refuses at step construction, so the judge never sees one.
- Command-surface name chosen: /remote — the slice own subject word, in the one-word lowercase house style of /multirepo, /rollback, /doctrine; checked first: no /remote surface existed in the tree (the only /remote-shaped hits are git refs/remotes prose in commit_craft.py), and no remote_work module name clashed.
- Built: mythic_agent/core/remote_work.py — REMOTE_WORK_SLICE = "M-114" + CONNECTION_KINDS (ssh — the one kind the roadmap names) + CONNECTION_PHRASES + AUTH_METHODS (agent / key / password) + AUTH_PHRASES + FILE_OP_KINDS (read / write / edit / list) + FILE_OP_PHRASES, each kind said in plain words and each read through a normalize function with short spellings, any other word refusing (in particular there is no download or copy file operation: files stay on that machine) + RemoteConnection frozen dataclass (also exported as Connection: kind via normalize_connection_kind, auth via normalize_auth_method, identity_file naming the key file when and only when the key road is named — key auth with no key file refuses, and a key file under any other auth refuses, so the description never disagrees with itself) + validate_connection / build_connection + RemoteTarget frozen dataclass (also exported as RemoteHost, Target and Host: target_id via normalize_target_id, address via normalize_address — a hostname or address word, the port never riding inside it — user via normalize_user, port via normalize_port (a whole number 1..65535, a digit word read as the number, a boolean refusing; DEFAULT_PORT 22), workspace_root via normalize_workspace_root — an absolute path ON the remote machine held as data, never resolved here — label defaulting to the target id, connection coerced through validate_connection, connection_line as the user@address:port data line) + validate_target / build_target + RemoteCommand frozen dataclass (also exported as Command: argv through the M-103 _check_argv rule carried whole — a single string refuses as a shell string, an empty list and non-text or empty words refuse — optional cwd as a remote path, label defaulting to the command text for display) + validate_command / build_command + RemoteFileOp frozen dataclass (also exported as FileOp: kind via normalize_file_op_kind, path as a remote path naming no empty or parent segment and no backslash, local_copy a real bool recording honestly, as data, whether the operation would copy the file back to THIS machine so the judge can refuse it plainly) + validate_file_op / build_file_op + validate_step (a mapping naming both a command and a file operation refuses as ambiguous rather than being guessed at) + RemoteWorkPlan frozen dataclass (also exported as WorkPlan and Plan: name as clean text, target_id via normalize_target_id, a non-empty ordered tuple of validated steps, command_steps / file_steps derived, stays_on_remote derived as no file step naming a local copy, plan_id via normalize_plan_id) + validate_work_plan / build_work_plan + judge_work_plan, the mechanical judge (the plan ONE target as a RemoteTarget or as a RemoteWorkRegistry holding targets: a mismatched target refuses, an unknown registry target refuses plainly naming the targets actually held or that none are held; a file operation naming local_copy refuses plainly — files stay on that machine; a file path or command cwd naming somewhere outside the target remote workspace root refuses, the root itself and relative work under it allowed; the judged plan is returned unchanged — judging opens no connection and runs nothing) + plain_words (a staying plan stated with its counts and that every file and command stays on that machine; a copying plan stated as one that would bring a file back and refuses as named, never as ready) + remote_work_record returning the session-news dict (type remote_work_plan, news true, plan id, name, target, every step as data, command and file-op counts, stays_on_remote, and the plain-words headline) + RemoteWorkRegistry, an in-memory storage-neutral registry model (register_target / get_target refusing plainly for unknown ids / list_targets in registration order; register / build_and_register — built naming the one target and judged in one move, a refusal holding nothing — / get raising KeyError for unknown plan ids / list_plans / record; caller-supplied plan ids used as given with duplicates refused, unsupplied ids the deterministic sequence plan-1, plan-2, ... skipping numbers a supplied id already took — no randomness a test cannot pin) + PLAIN_EXPLANATION + USAGE and the shared parse_remote_args for the /remote surface (empty/show -> ("show", None); targets -> ("targets", None); show <plan> or a bare plan id -> ("show", plan); a grammar word in the wrong shape is a USAGE error, never a plan id) and formatters (format_target with the one connection and the remote root stated whole in words, format_targets with the plain "no targets held" empty statement, format_work_plan / format_plan with step counts always stated and each step shown on its target, format_plans, format_remote_record / format_record, format_remote_screen the whole slice — status stated in words wherever shown, never colour or symbol alone). The module holds targets, connections and plans as data only: it opens no connection, runs no subprocess, writes no file, changes no setting, and imports no agent / UI code (stdlib dataclasses / typing only).

the vocabulary is deliberately NOT closed, any plain key word is a choice so long as it states its effect) + ApprovalChoice frozen dataclass (also exported as Choice: key via normalize_choice_key, label and effect as soft text — empty is representable because emptiness is what the judge reads) + validate_choice / build_choice (mapping aliases key/name, effect/does; conflicting spellings and unknown keys refuse) + ApprovalMoment frozen dataclass (also exported as Moment: what / where / what_it_does as soft text, choices as validated choices with distinct keys, question_count a whole number of at least one, record_id via normalize_record_id; text_size() for the phone-sized rule; to_record()) + validate_moment / build_moment (aliases what/question, where/location/path, what_it_does/does/effect; a questions list names the question count and a single entry answers what; conflicting counts and unknown keys refuse) + MomentAssessment frozen dataclass (also exported as Assessment: state validated against its own evidence on construction — complete names nothing missing, incomplete names at least one; missing held in the fixed order required parts, then choice_effects, single_question, phone_sized) + judge_moment, the mechanical judge (reads the proposal only: a missing what/where/what_it_does names that part; fewer than two choices names choices; any choice with no label or no stated effect names choice_effects; a stated question count above one or a what asking more than one question names single_question; a whole text over the budget names phone_sized; nothing missing is complete — the judge invents no context, choice or effect) + plain_words (a complete assessment restates the one question with its full context; an incomplete one names every missing part and says the moment is not ready to ask) + moment_record (session-news dict: type approval_moment, session_event approval_moment, news true, slice, state, the moment whole, missing, reasons, plain-words headline) + ApprovalMomentRegistry (also exported as Registry: in-memory, storage-neutral; deterministic approval-moment-N ids following the grace-N pattern — caller-supplied ids used as given with duplicates refused, unsupplied ids skip numbers a supplied id took; judge_and_register / get raising KeyError / list_records / record) + PLAIN_EXPLANATION + USAGE + shared parse_moment_args for the /moment surface (empty/show -> ("show", None); parts -> ("parts", None); show <record> or a bare record id -> ("show", record); a grammar word in the wrong shape is a USAGE error, never a record id) + formatters format_choice / format_parts / format_moment / format_assessment / format_moment_record (format_record) / format_moment_screen (a choice with no stated effect is shown as not ready, never dressed up).

- Built in team-02 on the M-116 pure layer (880c2e2), banked base 11dd1fe (115/272). Agent layer only — no push, no merge.

Methods: `approval_moment_status()` (held records + screen + PLAIN_EXPLANATION; reading judges/registers/publishes nothing), `get_approval_moment(record_id)` (unknown id raises KeyError, as the pure registry answers), `register_approval_moment(assessment)` and `judge_approval_moment(proposal)` — each holds via the pure registry and publishes the pure `moment_record` through `_publish_approval_moment_record`, journaled via `_checkpoint` as an `approval_moment` session event, deduped per record id (`_approval_moment_announced`); a refusal (duplicate id, bad proposal) holds nothing and publishes nothing.
- Live-road composition, honestly: `approval_moment_for_call(name, arguments)` shapes a moment from the real call the ToolPolicy ask road carries, and from nothing else — what is the call itself ("Run <name>?"); where is named only when the arguments themselves name a place (path / file_path / file / directory / cwd / root / destination); what_it_does is named only when the arguments themselves carry the act (command / cmd); the two choices are the road's own (approve: "the <name> call runs"; deny: "the <name> call does not run"). A part the real call does not name is left empty, so `judge_moment` honestly names it missing — no context, transport or road is invented. `judge_approval_call(name, arguments)` = shape + judge + hold + publish in one move. In `Agent.bind_tui`, the approve callback calls the guarded `_note_approval_moment(name, arguments)` after the M-096 remembered-approval shortcut (a standing answer raises no fresh ask, so it shapes no moment) and before `prompt_approval_sync`: shaping is news, never a gate — a shaping failure is swallowed there and can never turn an ask into a refusal; the prompt road decides exactly as before. Honest boundary: the terminal road's own approve callback (`terminal.configured_agent`) and `command_handler._policy`'s prompt road call `prompt_approval_sync` directly without `bind_tui`, so asks raised there shape no moment in this layer; nothing is claimed for them.

- Policy file format: YAML, filename mythic-policy.yaml at the repo root (POLICY_FILENAME; chosen because the file must sit where the repo's own tooling and reviewers look first - the repo root, beside the checkout it governs - and the mythic- prefix keeps it from colliding with generic policy.yaml names other tools claim). Schema mythic.policy_file/1. Top-level keys are exactly schema / name / posture / jail / hooks; the jail section's keys are exactly enabled / grants. A shipped example/template rides as data at mythic_agent/data/policy_example.yaml (EXAMPLE_RESOURCE, loaded via the house load_resource road) in the red_team_pack.yaml house data style.
- Built: mythic_agent/core/policy_file.py - Policy / PolicyPlan frozen dataclasses + validate_policy raising the house ValidationError (from core.validation) + load_policy_file (missing file -> None, stated plainly; malformed YAML and every refusal raise ValidationError naming the file; an empty file is an empty policy) + load_example_policy + application_plan (alias build_plan), the mechanical judge deriving the settings-shaped plan as data only + plain_words + policy_record (session-news dict, type project_policy) + PolicyRegistry with deterministic policy-N ids in the keeper pattern + the shared /policy parser (show / plan / sections) and formatters (format_policy / format_plan / format_policy_screen). Composition is by import, not copy: posture validates through M-095's profile_named / validate_profile, grants through M-099's build_grants, hooks through M-101's validate_hooks, and the plan's keys are the roads' own SETTING_KEY / GRANTS_KEY constants - no second copy of any rule exists to drift. NO deviation from reuse was needed.
- The never-loosen rule, exactly as enforced (recorded per the slice brief): a checked-in policy (1) may not turn the path jail off - jail.enabled: false refuses, because the file travels to every checkout silently while /pathjail off remains a live person's interactive choice; (2) may not grant the filesystem root - a root grant nullifies the jail the file claims to set. Everything else is bounded by the shipped roads' own validators: unknown posture names refuse (M-095), malformed custom postures refuse (M-095, incl. impersonating a shipped id), non-absolute or malformed grants refuse (M-099), hook records M-101 refuses refuse, and unknown top-level or jail keys refuse (a policy file must not silently invent settings). When in doubt the layer refuses rather than loosens.
- No-file / empty behaviour, stated plainly: no mythic-policy.yaml = no policy and no plan (load returns None; formatters say so in words); an empty policy = an empty plan. Two pure tests added for the extension. The never-loosen rule survives untouched: the plan shape still cannot turn the jail off, and no new road around it was added.
- Built (agent layer, mythic_agent/agents/llm.py): _policy_registry() holding a session PolicyRegistry in _policy_store (session state only, NO new setting) + per-policy application outcomes in _policy_outcome_store; project_policy_path() resolving mythic-policy.yaml at the Agent's real workspace root (self.project_root, via the pure default_policy_path); policy_on_disk() (missing file -> None; invalid file -> the pure ValidationError); get_project_policy() (unknown id -> KeyError, as the pure registry answers); project_policy_status() (held policies with their application outcomes, the on-disk policy or plain no-policy state or refusal reason, the shared screen, the plain explanation - a read that applies/registers/publishes nothing); set_hooks() (see deviations); apply_project_policy() + _apply_policy_plan(); _publish_policy_record() (the pure policy_record enriched with policy_id + per-step outcomes + applied flag, journaled via _checkpoint, deduped per policy record id in _policy_announced); _publish_policy_refusal(); and _load_project_policy_at_start(), called from attach_session once the session is attached (the session-start load the pure layer owed), failure-isolated exactly like the salvaged-restore road so a policy failure never breaks a session.
- Load/apply semantics: the file is loaded WHOLE through load_policy_file before any step runs. No file = status no_policy, nothing applied, nothing registered, nothing published, no invented default. An invalid file = status refused: no step runs, settings provably unchanged (pinned by test), the refusal is published as a project_policy_refused session-news record carrying the validator's reason, and the surface says "refused whole" plainly. A valid policy is registered (policy-N), and its plan is applied in the FIXED order posture, jail, grants, hooks, ONLY through the shipped roads: set_permission_profile (M-095), set_path_jail + add_path_grant (M-099), set_hooks (the M-101/M-102 storage road). Each road keeps its own journal-first + save-failure-revert behaviour; nothing writes settings behind their backs.
- MULTI-STEP FAILURE RULE FOLLOWED (stated per the brief): the change-set / patch-exchange per-unit precedent - steps run in the fixed order and one step's refusal or save failure does NOT stop the remaining steps; that step's own road revert governs that step alone, the failure is recorded in the policy record's steps with its reason, and the overall outcome is "partial" (record field applied: False) when any step failed, "applied" when every step applied or was already satisfied. A step the live settings already match exactly is recorded "satisfied" and is never forced through a road that refuses no-ops (set_path_jail refuses an already-on jail; add_path_grant refuses a duplicate grant) - satisfaction is judged on the raw stored value / the roads' own readers before calling. Pinned by test: a hooks-step save failure reverts the hooks exactly (hooks_reverted journaled), the posture and grants steps stand, the outcome is partial, and the published record carries applied: False.
- Surfaces: /policy on BOTH roads sharing the pure parse_policy_args + formatters - terminal.py (show = project_policy_status screen; plan = the data-only plan for the file on disk, applying nothing; sections = format_policy_sections; apply = apply_project_policy screen; help line gained /policy [show|plan|sections|apply]) and command_handler.py (_handle_policy in the sibling style, routed beside /moment), plus chat_screen.py help line, usage entry, and system-command routing entry mirroring /posture, /pathjail, /hooks.
- DEVIATIONS, recorded plainly: (1) No wholesale set-hooks Agent road existed - install_hook_pack (M-102) only merges shipped packs, and /hooks has no set verb - so the hooks step rides a new Agent.set_hooks built as an exact copy of the install road's shape (M-101 validation before mutation, journal-first hooks_changed, plain records under the hooks setting, save-failure restore + hooks_reverted + RuntimeError, already-identical = changed False with no journal and no save, the M-102 already-installed precedent). (2) No remove-grant road exists in M-099, so the grants step ENSURES each policy grant through add_path_grant (skipping grants already named as satisfied); a pre-existing grant the policy does not name is not removed, because no shipped road removes one. With the shipped defaults (no grants) the applied settings equal the plan exactly, as the tests pin. (3) The grants step's status is "applied" when every grant applied or was satisfied, with per-grant outcomes carried in the step's grants list.
- Tests: tests/test_policy_file.py extended in the sibling style - 19 new tests (2 pure parser/formatter extension tests; registry/status incl. one-registry-per-agent and the _policy_store attribute name; no-file = plain no-op with config unchanged; valid policy on a real temp workspace applying through the real roads with live re-proofs - the real M-095 decide() denies commands under the policy posture and the real M-099 check_path_jail() allows the granted path and denies an outside path; policy_record published exactly once per record id via the checkpoint road, a second apply registering policy-2 and publishing its own record with all steps satisfied; session-start load on attach_session; invalid file (jail-off) refused whole with settings provably unchanged and one project_policy_refused event; hooks save-failure partial with revert; set_hooks replace/no-op/refusal; /policy on the terminal road incl. help line and usage-error text; /policy on the TUI command-handler road; chat_screen routing). A successful spawn publishes the member's casting_record as session news, deduped per member id - a member that was registered and then spawned is announced exactly once (pinned by test with a real SessionStore).

A member that arrived as a file alone - its record names no shipped sub-agent entry, and a member record carries no prompt prose (the pure layer refuses a prompt key in a member mapping) - cannot be spawned from its record alone. spawn_crew_member reports that outcome plainly (spawned: False with the reason naming the missing entry) and invents no agent, no prompt and no road; such a member spawns once its name is carried as a sub-agent entry. Pure layer only; no push, no merge, the main checkout at mythic-hammer/repo untouched, no README touched, no config_defaults / config_catalog / PUBLIC_API / OWNERSHIP / CAMPAIGN_STATE / WAVE_PLAN or roadmap files touched. Only targeted/neighbour suites were run, never the full suite.
- Agent-layer decision: an agent layer IS owed for M-123 (the M-121/M-122 precedent). The custom-office machinery only bites when something live reads it: the owed agent layer holds a session CustomOfficeRegistry beside the session OfficeRegistry, offers the /offices custom surface on both roads sharing this layer's parser + formatters, and publishes custom_office_record as session news when a custom office is held. This pure layer deliberately wires nothing live, so the slice is NOT whole until that layer lands.
- Shipped roads inspected before building (composition, not duplication): core/offices.py + data/offices.yaml (M-122 - the formalised six: OfficeDefinition / HandoffContract dataclasses, validate_office / build_office, load_offices_file with hand-off closure held, OfficeRegistry, the /offices parser + formatters, PLAIN_EXPLANATION), core/crew_members.py (M-121 - the sibling pure-module shape: frozen validated dataclasses, alias _pick readings, ValidationError book loader, session-news record, registry, shared parser + formatters), core/role_routing.py (M-029 - office_ids, the shipped office vocabulary the no-collision rule is held against), the Agent layer in agents/llm.py for offices (session OfficeRegistry, office_record via _checkpoint - read for the owed layer's shape only; nothing wired), tests/test_offices.py (the sibling test shape), and core/scheduled_work.py for house docstring style.
- KEY CONSTRAINT found in the inspected code, and the design it forced: M-122's validators are closed over the six by design - normalize_formal_office refuses every other office, and OfficeDefinition / HandoffContract validate through it - so a custom office cannot be an OfficeDefinition and its machinery cannot simply be re-run on a new id. M-123 therefore composes with M-122 at the level the machinery honestly allows: the text / words / tools validators (_clean_text, _check_words, _check_tools) are M-122's own, imported and re-used unchanged (never duplicated), formalised hand-off targets are read through M-122's normalize_formal_office, and the custom dataclasses mirror the M-122 shape field for field. No M-122 code was changed.
- NAMESPACE DECISION (recorded per the slice brief): custom offices live in a plainly separate namespace, enforced by refusal, not by a prefix. A custom office id must not name ANY shipped office - not one of the six formalised offices, not the shipped polymath / guardian crew offices, and never primary (the lead session itself) - and the refusal names the boundary it hit. A custom office therefore cannot silently collide with, shadow, or override a formalised office, and a formalised OfficeDefinition offered to the custom machinery refuses plainly (it is already held by M-122). Hand-off targets are the one widening: a contract may land on a formalised office (read through M-122) or on a custom office defined in the same book - closure is held over that widened graph, never beyond it (a target that is neither refuses the book).
- Built: mythic_agent/data/custom_offices.yaml (schema mythic.custom_offices/1, slice M-123, part 6 "Agents as files", custom_note stating the shape, the no-collision rule and the examples-not-formalisation boundary) holding the roadmap's two named examples in order - gatekeeper (title Gatekeeper, focus "security review"; duties: security review of a built change, naming every risk with evidence, a plain gate verdict; tools read_file / list_dir / grep_search / run_command; hand-offs to auditor and forge_worker) and benchmarker (title Benchmarker, focus "benchmarking & measurement"; duties: run the benchmark pack, compare with the previous run, report numbers as measured; same tool set; hand-offs to auditor and to gatekeeper - the shipped custom-to-custom edge that proves closure over the widened graph in the example book itself). Every example tool is a shipped get_agent_tools name, pinned by test.
- Built: mythic_agent/core/custom_offices.py - CustomHandoffContract / CustomOfficeDefinition / CustomOfficesBook frozen dataclasses + validate_custom_office / build_custom_office (alias spellings id/office with the two-spellings refusal; unknown keys refuse; self hand-off and duplicated targets refuse; a formalised OfficeDefinition refuses) + validate_custom_offices (problem list; duplicate custom offices refuse; hand-off closure always held over formalised-plus-same-book-custom; examples mode also pins the shipped book to exactly gatekeeper + benchmarker in order) + load_custom_offices (the shipped file held to its examples) raising the house ValidationError + load_custom_offices_file (any book file on disk via the strict house YAML loader; examples NOT required, closure still held, so a file defining one new custom office loads - the user-defined road) + custom_office_record (session-news dict, type custom_office, formal False / custom True stated in the record itself, with plain headline) + plain_words + CustomOfficeRegistry (registration order, keyed by custom id, duplicates refuse, formalised books/definitions refuse) + normalize_custom_office / normalize_handoff_target / is_custom_office_id + the shared /offices custom parser (show / bare custom id / handoffs <office> / contract <from> <to>, the from-office custom, the target formalised or custom) and formatters (format_custom_office / format_custom_offices / format_custom_offices_screen) + PLAIN_EXPLANATION + EXAMPLE_CUSTOM_OFFICES naming the shipped examples in code. Parser note: a bare valid custom id parses (the custom namespace is open, so existence is the book/registry's judgement, not the parser's); a formalised or shipped id in the custom grammar refuses to the USAGE error. The module spawns no agent, runs no model, opens no session, imports no agent/UI code.
- Tests: tests/test_custom_offices.py, header TIER: integration in the sibling pattern (the shipped data file is part of its surface from the start): 22 tests - constants + usage; the examples are the roadmap's two and neither is a shipped office; word readings; the no-collision refusal for every shipped office (all six + polymath + guardian + primary, case variants) and malformed ids; hand-off target readings (formalised via M-122, custom, shipped-outside-the-six refusals); the shipped book loads and holds exactly the examples in order with the boundary stated in its note; the examples carry the roadmap charges (Gatekeeper/security review, Benchmarker/benchmarking, at least three duties each); every example tool is a live shipped tool name; the example hand-off graph is closed and exactly the four named pairs incl. benchmarker->gatekeeper; definition construction + record (formal False / custom True pinned); validator refusals (every shipped office as a custom id, blank title, empty/duplicated duties, empty/string/duplicated/malformed tools, empty inputs/outputs, self hand-off, duplicated target, unformalised target, missing contract field, unknown contract key); mapping aliases and the two-spellings refusal; a formalised OfficeDefinition refusing as custom at validate/build/registry; book refusals (wrong schema/slice, empty offices, unknown key, duplicate office, dangling custom hand-off, a formalised office as a book entry, examples-mode gaps); THREE files-on-disk proofs (the two-example roundtrip, a single new custom office handing off to a formalised office, missing/malformed/formal-entry file refusals); the registry incl. the shipped book, duplicate refusals and formalised-book refusal; the session-news record; plain words + formatters; the parser incl. the open-namespace bare-id reading; and a module-purity pin (which also pins that the M-122 validators are composed by import).

## M-126 agent layer DONE (2026-11-07 keeper worker, team-02)
- Built in team-02 on the M-126 pure layer (72805f1) and its TEAM_LOG commit (6583699), banked base df7630e = M-125 closure (125/272), verified by git before starting (HEAD exactly 6583699 on 72805f1 on df7630e, branch hammer/team-02, clean). Agent layer only; no push, no merge, the main checkout at mythic-hammer/repo untouched, no README touched, no config_defaults / config_catalog / PUBLIC_API / OWNERSHIP / CAMPAIGN_STATE / WAVE_PLAN or roadmap files touched. Only targeted/neighbour suites were run, never the full suite.
- Design, in the M-122..M-125 shape: the Agent holds a session CrewCommsRegistry behind _crew_comms_registry(), created lazily on first use, the attribute named _crew_comms_store, session state only, NO new setting. The status/read roads (crew_comms_status / get_handoff / find_handoffs / comms_ledger / human_question_batch) register, publish and mutate nothing - pinned against a real SessionStore; the find road reads the held registry first when the session holds any hand-off and falls back to the shipped example ledger only while nothing is held (a held session answers for its own traffic: a member holding nothing there raises KeyError, no silent fall back; the human reads as a recipient, so finding the human's hand-offs finds every hand-off addressed to the human). The holding roads (register_handoff / record_comms_ledger) publish each held hand-off's handoff_record as session news through the _checkpoint road via _publish_comms_record, deduped per hand-off id, foreign record types refused (task_dealing / crew_member / office / worktree_assignment records all pinned as refused); record_comms_ledger remembers the ledger as the session's ledger and refuses the same ledger recorded twice (fingerprinted on its hand-offs - a ledger is recorded once, never duplicated into the session). The batched ask is a road of its own (ask_batched_human_questions): the batch is derived from what the session holds and published as ONE human_question_batch session event holding every waiting question with its full context - never one event per question - deduped per batch through _publish_batch_record, so asking again while the same questions wait publishes nothing new, a batch grown by a newly held question announces as the new ask it is, and an empty batch publishes nothing. NO crew spawn, NO delivery road and NO board in this layer either - the pure layer's boundary holds: the registry holds hand-offs as data, no message is delivered, Part VIII does not exist yet.
- Surfaces: /comms on BOTH roads sharing the pure layer's parse_comms_args + format_handoffs / format_human_batch / format_kinds / format_comms_screen - terminal.py (show = the crew_comms_status screen; show <member> / bare member id / human = that member's hand-offs; ask = the one batched ask; kinds = the three kinds; the terminal /help line gained /comms [show|show <member>|ask|kinds]) and command_handler.py (_handle_comms in the sibling style, dispatched beside _handle_dealing), plus chat_screen.py help line, usage entry and system-command routing. No second parser was invented, and NO pure-layer change was needed in this layer.
- Tests: tests/test_crew_comms.py extended in the sibling style - 21 new tests (status: unheld-but-example screen with the example batch + one-registry-per-agent with the _crew_comms_store attribute name + reads registering/publishing nothing against a real SessionStore + unknown-member KeyError + status after register; register/record: publish-once with dedup and foreign-type refusal on both publish roads + record_comms_ledger holding and publishing all six example hand-offs + the same ledger recorded twice refusing + a ledger from a temp file recorded at the agent layer + find roads with example fallback and held preference; the ask: ONE batch event holding both questions, a repeat ask deduped, a grown batch announced as a second event, and an ask with no waiting questions publishing nothing + a no-delivery/no-board pin; terminal surface incl. bare-member and human forms, help line and usage-error text; TUI command-handler road incl. ask/kinds forms; chat_screen routing). The full suite was NOT run (the coordinator runs it at banking).

- Honest boundary, stated in the module, the data file, PLAIN_EXPLANATION and the tests: the veto as data only - verdicts are derived mechanically from GIVEN evidence (cases, checks, results) against the checks each slice carries; no check is run, no test suite is executed, no slice is banked or merged, and no Part VIII board exists or is claimed. No engine banking road exists in the shipped tree yet to wire the veto into (the banking discipline is M-205 / M-206, still queued), so nothing claims one was wired; the gate states are derived data the coordinator and the later banking road read.
- Built: mythic_agent/data/auditor_veto.yaml (schema mythic.auditor_veto/1, slice M-130, part 6, keeper auditor, ledger_note stating the shape and the boundary plainly) holding ONE EXAMPLE VETO LEDGER in the shape a real ledger's file takes: three cases, six checks and five results among the shipped Part VI slices, holding exactly one outcome of each kind - M-128 (Merge discipline, built by forge_worker) passed with both required checks proven, M-129 (Heterogeneous crews, built by scribe) vetoed on its suite-green check, which the Auditor's run failed (failed_check), and M-127 (Cycle and deadlock detection grown, built by cartographer) vetoed on its suite-green check, which holds no result at all (unproven_check) - pinned by the loader's example check, which also holds the derived gates to M-128 passed 2/2, M-127 vetoed with 1 proven + 1 unproven, and M-129 vetoed with 1 proven + 1 failed. A real veto ledger is a new file in this same shape, never this file edited in place.
- Built: mythic_agent/core/auditor_veto.py - AuditCase / AuditCheck / AuditResult / VetoVerdict / GateState / AuditorLedger / VetoReport frozen dataclasses + validate_case / validate_check / validate_result / validate_verdict / build_case / build_check / build_result / build_verdict (alias spellings slice_id/slice, builder/builder_id, name/check/check_name, outcome/result/status, evidence/note, with the two-spellings refusal on every field) + judge_veto (the mechanical judge: each required check earns at most one verdict - failed when its held result failed, unproven when it holds no result; failed verdicts first, then unproven, in case order; a check that is not required never vetoes, and passing is a gate state, not a verdict) + validate_ledger / load_auditor_ledger / load_auditor_ledger_file + AuditorVetoRegistry (deterministic case-N / check-N / result-N ids) + case_record / check_record / result_record / verdict_record / report_record session-news records + format_verdict / format_verdicts / format_gates / format_kinds / format_veto_screen + parse_veto_args, the shared /veto grammar. 43 public names in __all__.
- Bug found and fixed inside this slice's own scope during its first test run: validate_check and validate_result resolved a field's third spelling only when the first two were absent, so a record naming two different values under two spellings of one field (check vs check_name; outcome vs status) was silently read as the first instead of refusing, against the slice's own two-spellings law. Fixed with a _pick_any reading that refuses whenever any two spellings of one field disagree, and pinned by two refusal tests (check/check_name and outcome/status). The solo suite went from 42 passed + 1 failed to fully green on the fix.
- Tests: tests/test_auditor_veto.py created in the sibling style - 21 pure tests (constants and word readings incl. the M-121 auditor casting pin; case/check/result construction, records and refusals; validator aliases and the two-spellings refusals; the shipped example load, drift refusal and exactly-two-verdicts pin with gate states; the judge: clean evidence, kind-order precedence, optional checks never vetoing, stray evidence judging nothing; verdict and gate-state validation; the wrong-report naming; ledger construction and mapping refusals incl. the checkless-case refusal; the ledger file roundtrip; registry deterministic ids and refusals; records and plain words; formatters; the parser; module purity). The test file is banked with the agent-layer commit below, since its agent half exercises that layer; the pure half was run and green against this pure layer together with the agent layer in the same worktree (43 passed solo in total - see the agent entry).
- Owed coordinator deltas at banking: PUBLIC_API inventory (+ the 43 names in auditor_veto.__all__) + OWNERSHIP regen (a new core module + a new data file join the map), roadmap flip M-130 to done (130/272 at banking), ledger row; dead-code quarantine entries only if the banking gate flags programmatic-only surfaces (the reading is taken at banking, not assumed). NO config delta is proposed by the pure layer: the registry is session-held; any new setting is the agent layer's judgement, not assumed here.
- Still owed before M-130 can bank: the agent layer above, then the banking itself, in roadmap order (merge into the campaign main checkout -> full suite on the laptop -> PUBLIC_API + OWNERSHIP regen + any dead-code quarantine the gate flags -> closure commit -> explicit-refspec push -> ls-remote verify -> campaign state update). ## M-132 pure layer DONE (2026-11-07 keeper worker, team-08)
- Built in team-08 on banked base dc3ec75 = M-131 closure (131/272), verified by git before starting (HEAD exactly dc3ec75, branch hammer/team-08). Pure layer only; no push, no merge, the main checkout at mythic-hammer/repo untouched, no README touched, no config / PUBLIC_API / OWNERSHIP / CAMPAIGN_STATE / WAVE_PLAN or roadmap files touched. Only targeted/neighbour suites were run, never the full suite. Stall context: at the 23:20 EST keeper tick no team held a live session and no M-132 work existed on disk (the 23:2x M-131 banking note named M-132 next but restarted no build), so this keeper built M-132's pure layer itself in team-08, exactly as the 22:2x keeper did for M-131's pure layer in team-07. The M-131 confirmation suite was counted first this tick: 6611 passed / 2 failed / 1 skipped, 6614 collected (repo/logs/m131-fullsuite2.log) - the 2 failed are exactly the long-classified env cases (tiktoken-premise estimator; PortAudio public-api import).
- Design, in the M-131 research-swarms shape exactly: a panel is one change (PanelChange: title + plain-words summary) put to at most two reviews (PanelReview: reviewer as an M-121 member id composed by import from crew_members, provider + model as plain words in the M-129 heterogeneous-crew shape, a verdict of approve / request_changes / reject, plain-words notes, and named concerns, each once, possibly none). The outcome (PanelOutcome) is DERIVED, never stored: two identical verdicts yield agreed naming the shared verdict; two differing verdicts yield a disagreement naming both positions - no middle verdict, no score, no tie-break; fewer than two reviews yields awaiting naming who has reported. validate_panel judges the whole: at most two reviews, no reviewer twice, no model ridden twice (the roadmap's two-different-models word); the registry (ReviewPanelRegistry) holds one change and its reviews piece by piece with the same three refusals plus duplicate-id refusal, deterministic review-N ids. Records (change_record / review_record / outcome_record) are shaped as session news off the pieces' to_record; /panel grammar is shared via parse_panel_args (show / show <reviewer> / bare reviewer / outcome / verdicts / kinds) with format_review / format_reviews / format_outcome / format_verdict_kinds / format_panel_screen over PLAIN_EXPLANATION + USAGE. HONEST BOUNDARY: the panel is data only - no review is run, no model is called, no change is executed, no crew is spawned, and the Part VIII board does not exist.
- Shipped example (mythic_agent/data/review_panels.yaml, schema mythic.review_panels/1, held to its pinned shape by the loader's example check): the session-branching restore guard reviewed by auditor on anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-5 (approve, no concerns) and guardian on ollama/llama3.1 (request_changes, one concern: the refusal names no next step for the human) - the example outcome is the disagreement itself, surfaced. mythic_agent/core/review_panels.py holds 37 public names by live scan.

Two test-side slips found and fixed in this layer (a record round-trip through the validator - the sibling shape passes the dataclass itself - and a build_outcome field-word collision, now passed as a mapping) - no module defect. ## M-133 pure layer DONE (2026-11-08 keeper worker, team-09)
- Built in team-09 on banked base 520afd9 = M-132 closure (132/272), verified by git before starting (HEAD exactly 520afd98087c02688b05ad76ca16d9dcbf5a878d, branch hammer/team-09, clean except the preserved untracked m083-*.log files, left alone). Pure layer only; no push, no merge, the main checkout at mythic-hammer/repo untouched, no README touched, no config / PUBLIC_API / OWNERSHIP / CAMPAIGN_STATE / WAVE_PLAN or roadmap files touched. Only targeted/neighbour suites were run, never the full suite.
- Design, in the M-132 review-panels shape exactly: the shared project memory is the union view over per-member notebooks. A MemoryEntry is one fact in exactly one member's notebook (member as an M-121 member id composed by import from core/crew_members.normalize_member_id, never re-implemented; a short title; the fact in plain words; tags naming its topics, each once, possibly none; and its provenance). A MemberNotebook holds one member's entries in file order and judges: no foreign member's entry, no duplicate entry id inside it, no duplicate fact inside it (fact text compared case-insensitively with whitespace folded; the same fact in two DIFFERENT notebooks is two members' records and stands, pinned by test). A CrewMemory judges the whole: one notebook per member, entry ids unique across the whole memory, and its shared entries() view is derived in one deterministic order (member id, then entry id). FINDABLE BY ALL is search_memory / find_entries: exact + substring matching over entry ids, member ids, titles, fact text and tags, every query word required to match, run over the union of all notebooks; an asking_member argument is validated and then deliberately does NOT narrow the search, so the auditor's fact is found when queried as the scribe - pinned by test. There are no embeddings, no model calls and no relevance scores. Search is DERIVED from the held entries at call time, never stored: no record holds a search result or an index. The registry (CrewMemoryRegistry) holds notebooks entry by entry with deterministic entry-N ids for id-less entries, refusing a duplicate entry id anywhere and (through the notebook judge) a duplicate fact in one notebook and a second notebook for one member. Records (entry_record / notebook_record / memory_record) are shaped as session news off the pieces' to_record; /memory grammar is shared via parse_memory_args (show / show <member> / bare member / search <words> / tag <tag> / entry <entry-id> / members) with format_entry / format_entries / format_notebook / format_notebooks / format_memory_screen over PLAIN_EXPLANATION + USAGE. HONEST BOUNDARY: the memory is data only - nothing is persisted, synced, spawned or modelled here, and no Part VIII board is claimed.
- Shipped example (mythic_agent/data/crew_memory.yaml, schema mythic.crew_memory/1, held to its pinned shape by the loader's example check): three notebooks - the auditor's broken-checkpoint-chain fact (restoring a session branch refuses when the checkpoint chain is broken and names the broken link; provenance: the M-132 review panel example), the guardian's portaudio-env-import fact (the PortAudio public-api import failure is the long-classified environment case, not a code defect), and the scribe's verify-remote-moved fact (a banking push is proven only by git ls-remote showing the new head). mythic_agent/core/crew_memory.py (1020 lines) holds 30 public names by live scan of __all__ on the laptop; mythic_agent/data/crew_memory.yaml is 51 lines; tests/test_crew_memory.py is 367 lines.

Verification on the laptop (PYTHONPATH=$PWD, campaign .venv-hammer at ../repo/.venv-hammer): slice file 20 passed solo; neighbours tests/test_review_panels.py + tests/test_research_swarms.py + tests/test_crew_members.py + tests/test_crew_comms.py + tests/test_roadmap_slices.py + tests/test_arch_conformance.py together with the slice file 223 passed. No in-slice defects: the solo and neighbour runs were green on their first runs (one test-side wording fix before the first run: the deterministic-search query word was changed from 'fact', which no shipped fact text contains, to 'the', which all three contain).
- Owed at the agent layer (next step, in team-09): a session CrewMemoryRegistry on Agent in the M-132 shape (lazily held behind a _crew_memory_registry() road, the attribute named _crew_memory_store, never the method's own name, session state only). - Design, in the M-133 crew-memory shape exactly, with the M-131 digest rule at its heart: a stand-up report holds progress entries - one item of work per entry (member as an M-121 member id composed by import from core/crew_members.normalize_member_id, never re-implemented; a short title; the item in plain words; and the one state it stands in) - under a title and a boundary (the slice boundary the stand-up stands at, or an on-demand asking). The four states are the roadmap's own, canonical STATUS_KINDS (done, doing, blocked, needs_you) read through normalize_status with alias spellings (finished / complete / banked -> done, in-progress / working -> doing, stuck / held -> blocked, needs-you / needs-human / decision -> needs_you); two spellings of one field naming different values refuse, as in the siblings, and unknown keys refuse in an entry, a digest and the stand-up mapping. The DIGEST IS DERIVED, NEVER STORED: digest_stand_up groups the report's entries by state into the four sections in report order, an id-less entry refuses (a digest names every item it digests), StandUpDigest counts are read off its sections, and validate_digest(digest, report) refuses any stored digest that disagrees with the derived one - a digest named inside a stand-up file is judged the same way at load. NEEDS-YOU IS ITS OWN SECTION, never folded into blocked, so what needs Volmarr's word is always visible at a glance. The registry (StandUpRegistry) holds one report entry by entry in held order with deterministic entry-N ids for id-less entries, refusing a duplicate entry id and a second report under a different title or boundary. Records (entry_record / report_record / digest_record) are shaped as session news off the pieces' to_record; digest_plain_words renders the digest in plain words section by section (an empty section says nothing, plainly); /standup grammar is shared via parse_stand_up_args (show / digest / a state word / entry <entry-id> / kinds) with format_entry / format_entries / format_digest / format_status_kinds / format_stand_up_screen over PLAIN_EXPLANATION + USAGE. HONEST BOUNDARY: the stand-up is data only - nothing is scheduled, sent, posted, persisted or modelled here, and no Part VIII board is claimed.

- Built in team-10 on the M-134 pure layer (44cc026 + its TEAM_LOG 9e4ff54) atop banked base 6d294f9 = M-133 closure (133/272), verified by git before starting. Only targeted/neighbour suites were run, never the full suite.
- Design, in the M-133 crew-memory shape exactly: a session StandUpRegistry on Agent, lazily held behind _stand_up_registry(), the attribute named _stand_up_store, never the method's own name, session state only (with the _stand_up_held / _stand_up_recorded analogues of the M-133 pattern). Read roads (stand_up_status / get_stand_up_entry / stand_up_digest / stand_up) register, hold and publish nothing - pinned against a real SessionStore; they answer from the held registry first when the session holds any entry, and from the shipped example only while nothing is held (a held session answers for its own stand-up: an entry id the held report does not hold raises KeyError, no silent fall back). stand_up_status carries the held entries as plain records, the held report's record (none until one stands held), the session stand-up's pieces as plain records, its whole-report record, its derived digest and digest record, the one screen and PLAIN_EXPLANATION. Holding roads (register_stand_up_entry / record_stand_up_report) publish each held entry's entry_record as session news through the _checkpoint road via _publish_stand_up_record, deduped per entry id, foreign record types refused on this road (crew_memory_entry / member_notebook / panel_review / crew_member records all pinned as refused); a duplicate entry id refuses and publishes nothing new. record_stand_up_report(report=None) records a whole report once - the shipped example by default, or a report loaded from a file through the pure load_stand_up_file road - the same report recorded twice refusing (fingerprinted on its entry ids and boundary), publishing the entry records in report order and remembering the report as the session's. report_stand_up() publishes the held report's digest as ONE stand_up_digest (digest_record) session event via _publish_stand_up_report_record, deduped per whole digest shape (title, boundary, and the entry ids in each of the four sections), so a digest changed by a newly held entry announces again as the new digest it is. One honest adaptation the pure registry's shape requires, recorded plainly: the pure registry holds entries entry by entry but report() / digest() answer only once a title and boundary stand, so an entry held on its own is opened under a session stand-up (title Session stand-up, boundary session) by the agent layer; a whole report recorded first lends its own title and boundary to the entries held after it.
- /standup on both roads, sharing parse_stand_up_args and the pure formatters (format_entry / format_entries / format_digest / format_status_kinds): terminal.py road + help line, agents/command_handler.py _handle_standup + dispatch, ui/screens/chat_screen.py help line, usage entry and routing list. Grammar: show / digest / done / doing / blocked / needs-you / entry <entry-id> / kinds. NO config delta: the registry is session-held and no new setting was added.
- HONEST BOUNDARY: the stand-up is data only at this layer too - nothing is scheduled, sent, posted, persisted or modelled, and no Part VIII board is claimed; the digest is visible on this slice's own surfaces, not on a board.
- Tests: tests/test_stand_ups.py extended in the sibling style with 20 agent tests (status starts unheld but shows the example; the registry is one per agent; reads register and publish nothing against a real SessionStore; an unheld get raises KeyError; status after register shows the held pieces; a held session never falls back to the example; register publishes once plus foreign-type refusal for crew_memory_entry / member_notebook / panel_review / crew_member; duplicate id refusal publishes nothing new; record report holds and publishes the example as 5 entry events in report order; recording the same report twice refuses; a report from a file is recorded at the agent layer; the digest report publishes ONE stand_up_digest event and dedups; a changed digest announces again; no schedule / send / post / persist / model road exists on the agent; terminal show / digest / sections / entry / kinds plus the help line and bad grammar; command-handler show default, digest, section, entry, kinds and bad grammar; chat-screen routing). Pure layer only; no push, no merge, the main checkout at mythic-hammer/repo untouched (the hammer-bank-m134b confirmation suite running there was left undisturbed), no README touched, no config / PUBLIC_API / OWNERSHIP / CAMPAIGN_STATE / WAVE_PLAN or roadmap files touched. Only targeted/neighbour suites were run, never the full suite.
- Design, in the M-134 stand-ups shape exactly, with the M-134 derived-never-stored rule at its heart: a sizing book holds sizing cases - one task shape per case (its lead as an M-121 member id composed by import from core/crew_members.normalize_member_id, never re-implemented; its office as an M-122 formal office composed by import from core/offices.normalize_formal_office; a short title; the task in plain words; independent_pieces, the count of pieces the work splits into; and coupling, tight or loose, read through normalize_coupling with alias spellings: tightly-coupled / coupled / single-head -> tight, independent / parallel / splittable -> loose) - under a title. THE RECOMMENDATION IS DERIVED, NEVER STORED: derive_recommendation computes the size from the shape alone - tightly coupled work derives one pair of hands however many pieces it names (one pair of hands beats six when the work must be held in one head), loose work derives one pair of hands per independent piece capped at MAX_CREW_SIZE 6 - and the band (SIZE_KINDS solo / pair / crew / full_crew) is read off the size by size_kind_for, with SizingRecommendation refusing any band that disagrees with its size. A stored recommendation named in a case mapping, and any stored recommendation judged by validate_recommendation against its shape, refuses on any disagreement with the derived one. Two spellings of one field naming different values refuse, as in the siblings, and unknown keys refuse in a case, a recommendation and the sizing mapping. The registry (SizingRegistry) holds one book case by case in held order with deterministic case-N ids for id-less cases, refusing a duplicate case id and a second book under a different title. Records (case_record / book_record / recommendation_record) are shaped as session news off the pieces' to_record; recommendation_plain_words renders every case's derived size and reason in plain words (the solo case says plainly that one pair of hands beats six); /crewsize grammar is shared via parse_crew_size_args (show / recommend <case-id> / case <case-id> / a band word / kinds) with format_shape / format_shapes / format_recommendation / format_size_kinds / format_crew_sizing_screen over PLAIN_EXPLANATION + USAGE. HONEST BOUNDARY: the recommendation is data only - no crew is spawned, sized or modelled here, no model is called, and no Part VIII board is claimed.
- Shipped example (mythic_agent/data/crew_sizing.yaml, schema mythic.crew_sizing/1, held to its pinned shape by the loader's example check): the sizing book at the M-134 slice boundary, four cases covering all four bands - solo: the forge_worker's tangled-single-file-fix (6 pieces, tight, deriving 1: one pair of hands beats six); pair: the scribe's docs-and-tests-split (2 pieces, loose, deriving 2); crew: the architect's four-independent-slices (4 pieces, loose, deriving 4); full-crew: the cartographer's wide-census-sweep (9 pieces, loose, deriving the capped 6). mythic_agent/core/crew_sizing.py (1183 lines) holds 35 public names by live scan of __all__ on the laptop; mythic_agent/data/crew_sizing.yaml is 65 lines; tests/test_crew_sizing.py is 472 lines.

- Owed at the agent layer (next step, in team-01): a session SizingRegistry on Agent in the M-134 shape (lazily held behind a _crew_sizing_registry() road, the attribute named _crew_sizing_store, never the method's own name, session state only). Read roads (crew_sizing_status / get_sizing_case / sizing_recommendation / crew_sizing) register, hold and publish nothing - pinned against a real SessionStore; they answer from the held registry first when the session holds any case, and from the shipped example only while nothing is held (a held session answers for its own book: a case id the held book does not hold raises KeyError, no silent fall back). Holding roads (register_sizing_case / record_sizing_book) publish each held case's case_record as session news through the _checkpoint road via a _publish_crew_sizing_record helper, deduped per case id, foreign record types refused on this road (stand_up_entry / stand_up_digest / crew_memory_entry / panel_review / crew_member records all pinned as refused); record_sizing_book(book=None) records a whole book once - the shipped example by default, or a book loaded from a file through the pure load_crew_sizing_file road - the same book recorded twice refusing (fingerprinted on its case ids and title), publishing the case records in book order and remembering the book as the session's. report_crew_sizing() publishes the held book's derived recommendations as ONE crew_sizing_recommendation-set session event, deduped per whole recommendation shape, so a recommendation set changed by a newly held case announces again as the new set it is. Only targeted/neighbour suites were run, never the full suite.
- Design, in the M-135 crew-sizing shape exactly, with the fact/flavour separation at its heart where M-135 held the derived recommendation: a voice book holds voice profiles - one per member (its member as an M-121 member id composed by import from core/crew_members.normalize_member_id, never re-implemented; the voice's name; its register, read through normalize_register with alias spellings: flat / neutral -> plain, hearty / fireside -> warm, dry / deadpan -> wry, stern / stark -> grim; its signature markers as data; the greeting that opens the member's reports; the sign-off that closes them) - under a title, plus example reports. FACTS ARE CARRIED VERBATIM, FLAVOUR IS RENDERED, NEVER STORED LOOSE: render_report reads a report's flavour off its teller's profile (the profile's greeting and sign-off) and carries the source facts byte for byte; a stored report whose flavour disagrees with its profile, or whose facts disagree with their source facts by one character, refuses in validate_report - judged against the profile, the source facts, or both - exactly as a stored stand-up digest does (M-134). FLAVOUR INSIDE A FACT REFUSES: a fact carrying any of its teller's markers (checked case-insensitively) refuses in render_report, in validate_report against a profile, and at book build; and a profile holds no facts at all - a profile mapping naming facts refuses as an unknown key, because a voice frames facts, it does not own them. A report record holds its facts and its flavour in separate fields, pinned by test. One member holds one voice in a book: two profiles for one member refuse, as do duplicate profile ids and duplicate report ids, and a report naming a member the book holds no voice for refuses. Two spellings of one field naming different values refuse, as in the siblings, and unknown keys refuse in a profile, a report and the voices mapping. The registry (VoiceRegistry) holds one book voice by voice in held order with deterministic voice-N ids for id-less profiles, refusing a duplicate member, a duplicate profile id and a second book under a different title; it holds voices only - a report is a rendering derived at call time (registry render_report), never a holding. Records (profile_record / book_record / report_record) are shaped as session news off the pieces' to_record; report_plain_words tells every held report whole (flavour around verbatim facts); /voices grammar is shared via parse_voice_args (show / profile <member-id> / report <report-id> / a register word / registers) with format_profile / format_profiles / format_report / format_registers / format_character_voices_screen over PLAIN_EXPLANATION + USAGE. HONEST BOUNDARY: the voices are data only - no model is called, nothing is generated or rendered live, no member's code or facts are altered, and no Part VIII board is claimed.

- Built in team-03 on banked base 3475cd8 = M-136 closure (136/272), verified by git before starting (campaign repo HEAD and ls-remote of origin refs/heads/yrsa/feature-roadmap-build both exactly 3475cd8ab66b49ff30e3cd6940cf88ce9f1ea026; team-03 reset --hard to 3475cd8 by the keeper, clean except the preserved untracked m077-*.log files, left alone). Pure layer only; no push, no merge, the main checkout at mythic-hammer/repo untouched, no README touched, no config / PUBLIC_API / OWNERSHIP / roadmap data touched.
- Design, in the M-136 character-voices shape exactly, with the same-contracts-same-budgets rule at its heart where M-136 held the fact/flavour separation: a seat book holds external seats - one per outside agent (its member as a full M-121 crew member record composed by import from core/crew_members - validate_member / CrewMember, never re-implemented - so the seat's office, posture and tools are the contract, validated by the crew layer itself; its agent's own name; its home as one label; its transport, the single canonical word mcp with the model_context_protocol spelling read as mcp; and its budget as an M-107 budget composed by import from core/budgets - validate_budget / Budget, never re-implemented - validated by the budgets layer itself) - plus seat checks whose findings are DERIVED from the seat they judge (its office, its posture, its budget scope and limits in plain words), never stored loose: a stored check whose findings, verdict, member or transport disagree with the derived check refuses, exactly as a stored M-136 report does, and a check naming no findings judged against no seat refuses too. No foreign privileges: any seat key this layer does not know (a privilege, an exemption, a wider right) is an unknown key and refuses whole; a seat naming an office, a posture or a budget word the internal crew does not use refuses through those same internal validators. Alias spellings throughout (member/crew_member/member_record, agent_name/name/agent, home/server/origin, transport/road, seat_id/id, check spellings likewise) with the two-spellings-with-different-values refusal and the unknown-key refusal on seats, checks, the guide head and the top-level mapping. Registry: ExternalRegistry holds seats in held order with deterministic external-N auto ids, one seat per member, duplicate seat id refused, first book sets the title, checks derived at call time and never held. Records seat_record / book_record / check_record shaped as session news (type external_seat / external_seat_book / external_seat_check, slice M-137, news + headline). Plain words (plain_words per seat, check_plain_words per check or book), formatters (format_seat / format_seats / format_check / format_transports / format_external_agents_screen) and the shared parser parse_external_args for the /externals grammar.
- HONEST BOUNDARY, pinned by test: data and contracts only - the pure layer opens no MCP session, speaks no protocol, spawns no agent, calls no model, persists nothing and claims no outside agent is present or connected; the transport word mcp is data naming the Part X road, and the external agent's seat, contract and budget are data held to the same validators as internal crew. No Part VIII board is claimed; seats are visible through this slice's own /externals screen. The purity test bans live-road words in the module source (no subprocess/socket/requests/model SDKs, no mythic_agent.agents / mythic_agent.ui imports, no import mcp / mcp.client / fastmcp, no spawn/persist/schedule defs).
- Shipped example (mythic_agent/data/external_agents.yaml, schema mythic.external_agents/1, held to its pinned shape by the loader's example check): the seat book at the M-136 slice boundary, two outside agents - atlas_reviewer (Atlas Reviewer, home atlas-mcp, auditor office, read-only-scout posture, per-task budget of 20000 tokens / 40 commands) and ledger_hand (Ledger Hand, home ledger-mcp, scribe office, trusted-forge posture, per-session budget of $5.00 cost / 3600 seconds) - covering both budget scopes, plus one pinned check atlas-on-same-terms whose findings are pinned word-for-word. Both example members are NEW members (not in the shipped M-121 casting) whose contract and budget words are the shipped layers' own words, asserted by test against member_offices() / POSTURES / BUDGET_SCOPES.

- Design, in the M-137 external-agents shape exactly, with the derived-replay rule at its heart where M-137 held the same-contracts rule: a replay book holds journal steps - one per hand-off of the run (the hand-off itself as a full M-126 hand-off record composed by import from core/crew_comms - validate_handoff / Handoff, never re-implemented - so the sender, recipient, kind, slice, title and body are validated by the comms layer itself, member ids read by the M-121 layer's normalize_member_id by import; and its journal position) - under a title, plus decision traces whose account is DERIVED from the steps that led to the decision (every hand-off of the decision's slice, in journal order, up to and including the decision itself, one statement per step plus a closing statement), never stored loose: a stored trace whose decision step, member, slice, steps or account disagree with the derived trace refuses, exactly as a stored M-137 check does, and a trace naming no account judged against no steps refuses too. The replay itself is derived from the journal in order, never stored loose: a book's steps stand at positions 1..N in the order held, and a step naming any other position refuses as the book is built. Alias spellings throughout (step_id/id, position/seq/journal_position, handoff/hand_off/entry, trace spellings likewise: decision_step/step_id/step, member/decider, slice/slice_id, steps/positions, account/statements) with the two-spellings-with-different-values refusal and the unknown-key refusal on steps, traces, the guide head and the top-level mapping. Registry: ReplayRegistry holds steps in held order with deterministic replay-N auto ids, a step joining only at the next journal position, duplicate step id refused, first book sets the title, traces derived at call time and never held. Records step_record / book_record / trace_record shaped as session news (type crew_replay_step / crew_replay_book / crew_replay_trace, slice M-138, news + headline). Plain words (plain_words per step, trace_plain_words per trace or book), formatters (format_step / format_steps / format_trace / format_replay_screen) and the shared parser parse_replay_args for the /replay grammar.
- HONEST BOUNDARY, pinned by test: data only - the pure layer reads no live journal of a running crew, re-executes no step, delivers no message, spawns no agent, calls no model and persists nothing; a replay is a validated record of a finished run's hand-offs held as data, and a step's embedded hand-off record stays the comms layer's own record (its slice word is M-126). No Part VIII board is claimed; replays are visible through this slice's own /replay screen. The purity test bans live-road words in the module source (no subprocess/socket/requests/model SDKs, no mythic_agent.agents / mythic_agent.ui imports, no live-journal import, no spawn/persist/execute/rerun defs).
