Steps held one by one before any whole book stands recorded stand under a session replay book (title "Session replay book") composed from the held steps in the agent layer, so a later whole book recorded under its own title is never refused by a shim title at the registry (pinned by test); the journal order itself still governs what may join - a whole book recorded after one-by-one steps refuses, because a replay book always restarts at position 1 and a replay cannot restart mid-run, the one honest difference from the seat book's shape, pinned by test with its reason. The trace road replay_trace derives its trace at call time from the steps that led to the decision, held or shipped, so an account is never held stale; the registry holds steps and never traces, so a book registered whole answers with traces derived at call time, while a book recorded whole stands whole as the session's - its held example trace and all - through _crew_replay_held (pinned by test both ways). Holding roads register_replay_step / register_replay_book / record_replay_book publish each held step's step_record as session news through _checkpoint via _publish_crew_replay_record, deduped per step id, foreign record types refused on that road (character_voice_profile / crew_sizing_case / crew_sizing_recommendation_set / stand_up_entry / crew_memory_entry / panel_review / crew_member / external_seat / external_seat_book / external_seat_check / crew_replay_book / crew_replay_trace all pinned as refused); register_replay_book registers a whole book's steps through the registry in book order, while record_replay_book(book=None) records a whole book once - the shipped example by default, or a book loaded via the pure load_crew_replay_file road - the same book twice refusing (fingerprinted on its step ids + title), publishing the step records in book order and remembering the book whole as the session's, its held example trace and all, so the recorded example's trace still answers. Report road report_crew_replay renders the held book (or the shipped example when unheld) through the pure formatters alone (format_steps / format_trace / format_replay_screen) and registers and publishes nothing. The /replay surface rides BOTH roads (terminal + command_handler + chat_screen routing/help) sharing the pure parse_replay_args + pure formatters (format_step / format_steps / format_trace): show / step <step-id> / trace <step-id>. Honest boundary pinned by test: no generate / persist / execute / rerun / spawn / live-journal / board road exists on the agent - this layer reads no live journal, re-executes no step, persists nothing and calls no model.

Composition checked and NOT composed, with reasons: core/crew_comms hand-offs (M-126) - a failure report is not a hand-off, the hand-off kinds findings/diff/question carry no failure semantics, so this slice holds its own report record and composes the dealing instead; core/budgets (M-107) - a failure names no spend. Alias spellings throughout (report_id/id, member/member_id, slice/slice_id, failure_key/failure/key, body/text; assignment/dealing, report/failure_report, foster_member/foster, account/statements; report_ids/reports) with the two-spellings-with-different-values refusal and the unknown-key refusal on reports, re-deals, escalations, the guide head and the top-level mapping - the dealing layer's own alias spellings ride inside the embedded dealing, read by that layer. Registry: FosterRegistry holds dealings and reports in held order with deterministic fostering-N auto ids, duplicate slice/report id refused, first book sets the title, re-deals and escalations derived at call time and never held. Records report_record / redeal_record / escalation_record / book_record shaped as session news (type failure_report / failure_fostering_redeal / failure_fostering_escalation / failure_fostering_book, slice M-139, news + headline). Plain words (plain_words per report, redeal_plain_words and escalation_plain_words per record or book), formatters (format_report / format_reports / format_redeal / format_escalation / format_fostering_screen) and the shared parser parse_fostering_args for the /foster grammar.

No Part VIII board is claimed; fosterings are visible through this slice's own /foster screen. The purity test bans live-road words in the module source (no subprocess/socket/requests/model SDKs, no mythic_agent.agents / mythic_agent.ui imports, no journal import, no spawn/persist/execute/deliver/send defs).

- Design, mirroring the M-138 crew-replay agent layer exactly, adapted to a registry holding two record kinds: a session FosterRegistry on Agent, lazily held behind _failure_fostering_registry(), the attribute named _failure_fostering_store (never the method's own name), session state only - NO config delta, NO new setting. Read roads failure_fostering_status / get_failure_report / get_fostering_assignment / fostering_redeal / fostering_escalation (plus the book road failure_fostering()) register, hold and publish nothing (pinned against a real SessionStore); they answer from the held registry first when the session holds any dealing or report, and from the shipped example only while nothing is held - a held session answers for its own book alone: a report, dealing, slice or failure the held book does not hold raises KeyError (or, for an escalation the held reports cannot derive, ValueError) with no fall back, on every read road alike. The re-deal and escalation roads derive at call time from held records - fostering_redeal naming the fostering member, fostering_escalation naming one report of the failure - so an account is never held stale; the registry holds dealings and reports and never re-deals or escalations, so a book registered whole answers with re-deals and escalations derived at call time, while a book recorded whole stands whole as the session's - its held re-deal and escalation and all - through _failure_fostering_held (pinned by test both ways). Dealings and reports held one by one stand under a session fostering book (title "Session fostering book") composed from the held records in the agent layer, so a later whole book recorded under its own title is never refused by a shim title at the registry (a whole book recorded after one-by-one holdings still refuses, on the registry's own duplicate slice/report-id refusal, pinned by test). One honest difference from the M-138 shape, pinned by test: this registry holds two record kinds, so while only one side stands held - reports with no dealings, or dealings with no reports - no whole book can stand (a FosterBook requires at least one of each); failure_fostering() then returns None, the held records still answer on their own roads, and status and the report road render the held reports alone and claim no book. Report road report_failure_fostering renders the held book (or the shipped example when unheld) through the pure formatters alone (format_reports / format_redeal / format_escalation / format_fostering_screen) and registers and publishes nothing. The /foster surface rides BOTH roads (terminal + command_handler + chat_screen routing/help) sharing the pure parse_fostering_args + pure formatters (format_report / format_redeal / format_escalation): show / report <report-id> / redeal <slice-id> / escalation <report-id>; the surfaces' redeal word shows the re-deal the current book holds for that slice (the derivation itself names a fostering member and lives on the agent road fostering_redeal). Public names by live scan: mythic_agent/core/failure_fostering.py still carries exactly its 36 pure-layer names in __all__ (unchanged by this layer; the Agent methods are class methods, not __all__ names). Honest boundary pinned by test: no generate / persist / deliver / spawn / live-crew / board road exists on the agent - this layer re-deals no task to a live agent, delivers no escalation, reads no live crew, persists nothing and calls no model.

## M-140 pure layer DONE (2026-11-08 keeper worker, team-06)
- Built in team-06 on banked base 2b7dc03 = M-139 closure (139/272), verified by git before starting (team-06 HEAD exactly 2b7dc03f17154d6fe8e921e9ea2884ec2b20ee98 on branch hammer/team-06; the keeper ls-remote-verified the campaign branch at the same sha; team-06 clean except the preserved untracked m080-*.log files, left alone). Pure layer only; no push, no merge, the main checkout at mythic-hammer/repo untouched, no README touched, no config / PUBLIC_API / OWNERSHIP / roadmap data / CAMPAIGN_STATE / WAVE_PLAN touched.
- Design, in the M-139 failure-fostering shape exactly, with the derived-cost rule at its heart where M-139 held the derived-account rule: a court book holds run records in held order - the member who ran (M-121 member ids read by core/crew_members.normalize_member_id by import), the slice the run served (slice ids read by the M-124 worktrees layer's normalize_slice_id by import), the model and the input / output tokens - and names NO cost, because a record's cost is DERIVED from the shared rate table (core/costs.price, M-107-era costs layer, composed by import, never re-implemented - the same table providers/cost_ledger.py, Slice 046, prices its per-call records from, checked and composed in that spirit: one call priced as usage x model price; a model the table does not know prices at $0 and is counted unpriced, exactly as the shipped cost layers read unknown models, and a stored cost key on a record refuses as an unknown key). A per-member-per-slice report is DERIVED from that pair's records in held order - its entry ids, calls, token totals, cost, unpriced count and account read off the records - never stored loose: a stored report whose entries, totals, unpriced count or account disagree with the derived report refuses, and a report naming no account refuses too. The Scribe's ledger is DERIVED from the whole book: its rows are the per-member-per-slice reports in the order the records first name each pair, its totals are the rows' sums (a ledger whose totals are not its own rows' sums refuses at construction) and its account is read off the rows; a stored ledger whose keeper, rows, totals or account disagree with the derived ledger refuses, its rows are each judged against the book's records, and the keeper is always the Scribe - the shipped M-121 casting's own scribe member (SCRIBE_MEMBER), a ledger kept by anyone else refusing. Composition checked and NOT composed, with reasons: core/budgets (M-107) - a budget judges a limit against usage and answers with a verdict; a run record names no limit and the court delivers no verdict, so this slice composes the rate table instead of the budget judge; core/task_dealing (M-125) - a run record is not a dealing; the dealing layer's validators are not the record's shape, the member/slice normalizers are the composition. Alias spellings throughout (entry_id/id, member/member_id, slice/slice_id, input_tokens/input/prompt_tokens, output_tokens/output/completion_tokens; entry_ids/entries, calls/call_count, cost_usd/cost/spend, unpriced_calls/unpriced/unpriced_count, account/statements; keeper/scribe, rows/reports, the total_* names with their shorter spellings) with the two-spellings-with-different-values refusal and the unknown-key refusal on records, reports, ledgers, the guide head and the top-level mapping. Registry: CourtRegistry holds run records in held order with deterministic court-N auto ids, duplicate entry id refused, first book sets the title, reports and the ledger derived at call time and never held. Records entry_record / report_record / ledger_record / book_record shaped as session news (type cost_court_entry / cost_court_report / cost_court_ledger / cost_court_book, slice M-140, news + headline). Plain words (plain_words per record, report_plain_words and ledger_plain_words per record or book), formatters (format_entry / format_entries / format_report / format_ledger / format_cost_court_screen) and the shared parser parse_cost_court_args for the /costcourt grammar.
- HONEST BOUNDARY, pinned by test: data and contracts only - the pure layer calls no model, bills no one, charges nothing, reads no live crew run, spawns no agent and persists nothing; a cost here is a validated record held as data, derived from the shared rate table, and nothing here claims a crew run happened live or that Part XI's efficiency work exists - the Scribe's ledger is only the shape that work will read. The purity test bans live-road words in the module source (no subprocess/socket/requests/model SDKs, no mythic_agent.agents / mythic_agent.ui imports, no journal import, no spawn/persist/execute/deliver/send/charge/bill defs).

## M-140 agent layer DONE (2026-11-08 keeper worker, team-06)
- Built in team-06 on the M-140 pure layer (339dc28 + TEAM_LOG 6e78576, atop banked base 2b7dc03 = M-139 closure, 139/272; team-06 HEAD verified exactly 6e78576 atop 339dc28 atop 2b7dc03f17154d6fe8e921e9ea2884ec2b20ee98 before starting, the campaign branch verified at the same 2b7dc03 by the keeper at dispatch; team-06 clean except the preserved untracked m080-*.log files, left alone). Agent layer only; no push, no merge, the main checkout at mythic-hammer/repo untouched, no README touched, no config / PUBLIC_API / OWNERSHIP / CAMPAIGN_STATE / WAVE_PLAN or roadmap files touched. Only targeted/neighbour suites were run, never the full suite.
- Design, mirroring the M-139 failure-fostering agent layer exactly, adapted to a registry holding one record kind: a session CourtRegistry on Agent, lazily held behind _cost_court_registry(), the attribute named _cost_court_store (never the method's own name), session state only - NO config delta, NO new setting. Read roads cost_court_status / get_cost_entry / cost_report / scribe_ledger (plus the book road cost_court()) register, hold and publish nothing (pinned against a real SessionStore); they answer from the held registry first when the session holds any run record, and from the shipped example only while nothing is held - a held session answers for its own book alone: a record, member or slice the held book does not hold raises KeyError with no fall back, on every read road alike. The report and ledger roads derive at call time from held records - cost_report naming the member and the slice, scribe_ledger naming no argument - so a total is never held stale; the registry holds run records and never reports or ledgers, so a book registered whole answers with reports and the ledger derived at call time, while a book recorded whole stands whole as the session's - its held ledger and all - through _cost_court_held (pinned by test both ways). Records held one by one stand under a session cost court book (title "Session cost court book") composed from the held records in the agent layer, so a later whole book recorded under its own title is never refused by a shim title at the registry (a whole book recorded after one-by-one holdings still refuses, on the registry's own duplicate entry-id refusal, pinned by test). Holding roads register_cost_entry / register_cost_book / record_cost_book publish each held record's entry_record as session news through _checkpoint via _publish_cost_court_record, deduped per entry id, foreign record types refused on that road (cost_court_report / cost_court_ledger / cost_court_book / failure_report / crew_replay_step / character_voice_profile / crew_sizing_case / stand_up_entry / crew_memory_entry / panel_review / crew_member / external_seat all pinned as refused). register_cost_book registers a whole book's records through the registry in book order, while record_cost_book(book=None) records a whole book once - the shipped example by default, or a book loaded via the pure load_cost_court_file road - the same book twice refusing (fingerprinted on its entry ids + title), publishing the entry records in book order and remembering the book whole as the session's. Report road report_cost_court renders the held book (or the shipped example when unheld) through the pure formatters alone (format_entries / format_report / format_ledger / format_cost_court_screen) and registers and publishes nothing. The /costcourt surface rides BOTH roads (terminal + command_handler + chat_screen routing/help) sharing the pure parse_cost_court_args + pure formatters (format_entry / format_entries / format_ledger): show / entry <entry-id> / member <member-id> / slice <slice-id> / ledger; the surfaces' member word renders through format_entries' member filter and the slice word through the held book's entries_on read over format_entry, since the pure entries formatter filters by member alone. Public names by live scan: mythic_agent/core/cost_court.py still carries exactly its 36 pure-layer names in __all__ (unchanged by this layer; the Agent methods are class methods, not __all__ names). Honest boundary pinned by test: no generate / persist / bill / charge / live-crew / board road exists on the agent - this layer calls no model, bills no one, charges nothing, runs no live crew, persists nothing and claims no crew run happened live.
- Files: mythic_agent/agents/llm.py +381, mythic_agent/agents/command_handler.py +68, mythic_agent/terminal.py +39/-1 (costcourt road + help line), mythic_agent/ui/screens/chat_screen.py +3/-1 (help line, usage entry, routing), tests/test_cost_court.py +448 (939 insertions, 2 deletions across the five files). Committed locally in team-06 as 7444e7a - NOT pushed, NOT merged (banking is the coordinator's next step).

## M-141 pure layer DONE (2026-11-08 keeper worker, team-07)

Pure layer only; no push, no merge, the main checkout at mythic-hammer/repo untouched, no README touched, no config / PUBLIC_API / OWNERSHIP / roadmap data / CAMPAIGN_STATE / WAVE_PLAN touched.
- Roadmap words built to (roadmap_slices.yaml, M-141, Part VI The Crew): "the standing dogfood - each part of this road is built by the crew it improves, under the coordinator's discipline (as the October forge proved it can)."
- Design, in the M-140 cost-court shape exactly, with the derived-part rule at its heart where M-140 held the derived-cost rule: a dogfood book holds build records in held order - the member who built (M-121 member ids read by core/crew_members.normalize_member_id by import), the slice built (slice ids read by the M-124 worktrees layer's normalize_slice_id by import) and a title naming the build - and names NO part, because a record's part is DERIVED from the shipped roadmap (core/roadmap_slices.load_roadmap, composed by import, never re-implemented): a record naming a slice the road does not hold (e.g. M-999) refuses at construction, and a stored part key on a record refuses as an unknown key, so a record can never claim a part the road does not give its slice. A per-part report is DERIVED from that part's records in held order - its entry ids, builds, distinct slices, distinct members and account read off the records - never stored loose: a stored report whose fields or account disagree with the derived report refuses, and a report naming no account refuses too. The coordinator's ledger is DERIVED from the whole book: its rows are the per-part reports in the order the records first name each part, its totals are the rows' sums (builds summed, slices counted distinct across rows, parts counted as rows - a ledger whose totals are not its own rows' sums refuses at construction) and its account is read off the rows; a stored ledger whose keeper, rows, totals or account disagree with the derived ledger refuses, and the keeper is always the coordinator (COORDINATOR_KEEPER) - the discipline the roadmap words name, and NOT a casting member: the shipped offices note names the lead session itself the coordinator, never a working office, so the keeper word is this slice's own constant (asserted by test against load_casting()). Composition checked and NOT composed, with reason: core/costs + core/cost_court (M-140) - a build record names no spend and the dogfood delivers no price, so this slice composes the roadmap instead of the rate table. Alias spellings throughout (entry_id/id, member/member_id, slice/slice_id, title/name; entry_ids/entries, builds/build_count, slices/slice_ids, members/member_ids, account/statements; keeper/coordinator, rows/reports, the total_* names with their shorter spellings) with the two-spellings-with-different-values refusal and the unknown-key refusal on records, reports, ledgers, the guide head and the top-level mapping. Registry: DogfoodRegistry holds build records in held order with deterministic dogfood-N auto ids, duplicate entry id refused, first book sets the title, reports and the ledger derived at call time and never held. Records entry_record / report_record / ledger_record / book_record shaped as session news (type dogfood_entry / dogfood_report / dogfood_ledger / dogfood_book, slice M-141, news + headline). Plain words (plain_words per record, report_plain_words and ledger_plain_words per record or book), formatters (format_entry / format_entries / format_report / format_ledger / format_dogfood_screen) and the shared parser parse_dogfood_args for the /dogfood grammar.
- HONEST BOUNDARY, pinned by test: data and contracts only - the pure layer builds no slice live, runs no crew, calls no model, spawns no agent, merges or banks nothing and persists nothing; a build here is a validated record held as data, its part derived from the shipped roadmap, and nothing here claims a build happened live. The purity test bans live-road words in the module source (no subprocess/socket/requests/model SDKs, no mythic_agent.agents / mythic_agent.ui imports, no journal import, no spawn/persist/execute/deliver/send/merge/bank defs).
- Shipped example (mythic_agent/data/dogfood.yaml, schema mythic.dogfood/1, held to its pinned shape by the loader's example check): the dogfood book at the M-140 slice boundary - six build records in held order: entry-m120-forge (forge_worker, M-120, Part V gate), entry-m121-architect (architect, M-121, Agents as files), entry-m138-scribe (scribe, M-138, Crew replay), entry-m139-forge (forge_worker, M-139, Failure fostering), entry-m140-forge (forge_worker, M-140, Cost court) and entry-m140-auditor (auditor, M-140, Cost court) - plus one pinned ledger ledger-m140-boundary kept by the coordinator: rows part 5 (1 build, 1 slice) and part 6 (5 builds, 4 slices); totals 6 builds, 5 slices, 2 parts, its account and the first (part 5) report's account pinned word-for-word. Every example member - forge_worker, architect, scribe, auditor - is a shipped M-121 casting member, asserted by test against load_casting(), and every example part is asserted against the shipped roadmap's own slice parts by test.

- Built in team-08 on banked base e7481ca = M-141 closure (141/272), verified by git before starting (team-08 HEAD exactly e7481ca7303b360bba17a7e5eac16c0cd1940400 after the keeper's reset; team-08 clean except the preserved untracked .m082-logs/, left alone). Pure layer only; no push, no merge, the main checkout at mythic-hammer/repo untouched except reading, no README touched, no config / PUBLIC_API / OWNERSHIP / roadmap data / CAMPAIGN_STATE / WAVE_PLAN touched by this slice commit (the M-141 WAVE_PLAN entry was appended separately by the keeper's Task 1 this tick).
- Roadmap words built to (roadmap_slices.yaml, M-142, Part VI The Crew): "with the crew off, the single coder loses nothing - crew is an amplifier, never a requirement."
- Design, in the M-141 dogfood shape exactly, with the derived-parity rule at its heart where M-141 held the derived-part rule: a solo mode book holds capability records in held order - one capability the single coder holds (its capability word, a plain-words title and the detail of the work), read with M-121 member ids by core/crew_members.normalize_member_id by import - and names NO parity, because parity is DERIVED from the book itself: every record in the book is a capability held solo, so a stored crew_only / requires_crew / solo_holds key on a record refuses as an unknown key and no record can claim a capability exists only when the crew is on. A record may name the member who amplifies it when the crew is on and the gain that amplification brings; the two stand together or not at all, and the gain names what the crew ADDS (hands, eyes, speed), never the capability itself. A per-member amplification report is DERIVED from the records that member amplifies in held order - its capability ids, capability words, count and account read off the records - never stored loose: a stored report whose fields or account disagree with the derived report refuses, and a report naming no account refuses too. The parity ledger is DERIVED from the whole book: its rows are the per-member reports in the order the records first name each amplifying member, its totals are the records' own counts (every capability held, those amplified, those standing unamplified - a ledger whose amplified total is not its own rows' sum, or whose plain total is not the rest, refuses at construction) and its account is read off the rows and totals; a stored ledger whose rows, totals or account disagree with the derived ledger refuses. Composition checked: core/crew_sizing (M-135) was read and NOT composed - its solo is a size band (one pair of hands recommended by a task's shape), not a capability parity; no crew enable/disable setting exists in the shipped config defaults and no solo/crew-off handling exists anywhere in the tree (searched), so this slice composes the M-121 casting by import and nothing else. Alias spellings throughout (capability_id/id, capability/name, title/headline, detail/description, amplifier/member/member_id, gain/amplification; capability_ids, capabilities/capability_names, amplifications/count, account/statements; rows/reports, the total_* names with their shorter spellings) with the two-spellings-with-different-values refusal and the unknown-key refusal on records, reports, ledgers, the guide head and the top-level mapping. Registry: SoloRegistry holds capability records in held order with deterministic solo-N auto ids, duplicate capability id refused, first book sets the title, reports and the ledger derived at call time and never held. Records capability_record / report_record / ledger_record / book_record shaped as session news (type solo_capability / solo_amplification_report / solo_parity_ledger / solo_book, slice M-142, news + headline). Plain words (plain_words per record, report_plain_words and ledger_plain_words per report/ledger or book), formatters (format_capability / format_capabilities / format_report / format_ledger / format_solo_mode_screen) and the shared parser parse_solo_mode_args for the /solo grammar. Every example amplifier - architect, forge_worker, auditor, scribe - is a shipped M-121 casting member (crew_members.yaml).

- Design, mirroring the M-141 dogfood agent layer exactly, adapted to a registry holding one record kind whose reports and ledger are derived per member and from the whole book: a session SoloRegistry on Agent, lazily held behind _solo_mode_registry(), the attribute named _solo_mode_store (never the method's own name), session state only - NO config delta, NO new setting (none was added: no crew on/off setting exists in the shipped config defaults, as the pure layer already searched and recorded, so there is nothing for a setting to gate). Read roads solo_mode_status / get_solo_capability / solo_mode_report / parity_ledger (plus the book road solo_mode()) register, hold and publish nothing (pinned against a real SessionStore); they answer from the held registry first when the session holds any capability record, and from the shipped example only while nothing is held - a held session answers for its own book alone: a record or member the held book does not hold raises KeyError with no fall back, on every read road alike. The report and ledger roads derive at call time from held records - solo_mode_report naming the member, parity_ledger naming no argument - so a count is never held stale; the registry holds capability records and never reports or ledgers, so a book registered whole answers with reports and the ledger derived at call time, while a book recorded whole stands whole as the session's - its held ledger and all - through _solo_mode_held (pinned by test both ways). Records held one by one stand under a session solo mode book (title "Session solo mode book") composed from the held records in the agent layer, so a later whole book recorded under its own title is never refused by a shim title at the registry (a whole book recorded after one-by-one holdings still refuses, on the registry's own duplicate capability-id refusal, pinned by test).
- Holding roads register_solo_capability / register_solo_book / record_solo_book publish each held record's capability_record as session news through _checkpoint via _publish_solo_mode_record, deduped per capability id, foreign record types refused on that road (solo_amplification_report / solo_parity_ledger / solo_book / dogfood_entry / cost_court_entry / failure_report / crew_replay_step / character_voice_profile / crew_sizing_case / stand_up_entry / crew_memory_entry / panel_review / crew_member / external_seat all pinned as refused). register_solo_book registers a whole book's records through the registry in book order, while record_solo_book(book=None) records a whole book once - the shipped example by default, or a book loaded via the pure load_solo_mode_file road - the same book twice refusing (fingerprinted on its capability ids + title), publishing the capability records in book order and remembering the book whole as the session's. Report road report_solo_mode renders the held book (or the shipped example when unheld) through the pure formatters alone (format_capabilities / format_report / format_ledger / format_solo_mode_screen) and registers and publishes nothing. The /solo surface rides BOTH roads (terminal + command_handler + chat_screen routing/help) sharing the pure parse_solo_mode_args + pure formatters (format_capability / format_capabilities / format_report / format_ledger): show / capability <capability-id> / member <member-id> / report <member-id> / ledger; the surfaces' member word renders through format_capabilities' member filter, and the report word through solo_mode_report over format_report. The roster and index roads derive at call time from held records - crew_template_roster naming the formation, crew_template_index naming no argument - so a count is never held stale; the registry holds template records and never rosters or indexes, so a book registered whole answers with rosters and the index derived at call time, while a book recorded whole stands whole as the session's - its held index and all - through _crew_templates_held (pinned by test both ways). - Roadmap words built to (roadmap_slices.yaml, M-144, Part VI The Crew): "pausing one member, the whole crew, or one slice — each leaves its worktree and journal consistent."
- Design, in the M-143 crew_templates shape exactly, with the derived-consistency rule at its heart where M-143 held the derived-size rule: a crew interrupt book holds interrupt records in held order — one graceful pause (its scope word — member, crew or slice; a plain-words title; the detail of why it is taken; and the readings it rests on: the worktree as it stands — clean, checkpointed or dirty; the journal's flush state — flushed or pending; and the point the pause is taken at — a resting point or mid-edit) — and names NO consistency and NO status, because both are DERIVED from the readings at call time: the pause leaves its worktree and journal consistent exactly when the worktree is clean or checkpointed, the journal is flushed and the pause lands at a resting point, so a stored consistent / consistency / status / graceful / checks key on a record refuses as an unknown key and no pause can claim a grace its readings do not give it. Scope and target stand together: a member pause names exactly one member of the shipped M-121 casting and no slice, a slice pause names exactly one roadmap slice and no member, and a crew pause names neither — its target is the whole crew — and a record whose target disagrees with its scope refuses. A per-pause consistency report is DERIVED from the record's readings — its three checks (worktree, journal, pause point, each ending in its verdict), its consistency (true exactly when every check is consistent, cross-checked at construction) and its account read off the record — never stored loose: a stored report whose scope, target, checks, consistency or account disagree with the derived report refuses, and a report naming no account refuses too. The interrupt index is DERIVED from the whole book: its rows are the per-pause reports in held order, its totals are the records' own derived counts (every pause held, those leaving things consistent, those not yet) and its account is read off the rows and totals; a stored index that disagrees refuses. Validated records carry alias spellings throughout (scope/kind/target_kind, title/headline, detail/description/reason, worktree/worktree_state/tree_state, journal/journal_state, point/resting_point/pause_point, member/member_id, slice_id/slice, interrupt_id/id) with two-spellings-with-different-values refusals and unknown-key refusals on records, reports, indexes, the guide head and the book mapping alike; the InterruptRegistry holds records in held order with deterministic interrupt-N auto ids and duplicate-id refusals, reports and the index derived at call time, never held; records are shaped as session news (interrupt_record / report_record / index_record / book_record, each type + slice + news + headline); plain_words, the report and index plain-words forms, the formatters and the shared /interrupts parser are the pure layer's own.
- Composition decisions, grounded in what the shipped modules actually hold (checked on the laptop before composing anything): composed by import ONLY where the other layer truly holds the record vocabulary — core/crew_members.normalize_member_id (M-121: a paused member is the shipped casting's own member) and core/worktrees.normalize_slice_id (M-124: a paused slice is a roadmap slice id exactly as a worktree assignment reads one). - HONEST BOUNDARY, stated in the module, the data file and the tests: data and contracts only — no member is actually paused, no crew is halted, no process is signalled, no worktree is touched, no journal is written, nothing is persisted; an interrupt here is a validated record of a graceful pause, nothing more.

## M-145 pure layer DONE (2026-11-08 keeper worker, team-01)
- Built in team-01 on banked base eeff966 = M-144 closure (144/272), verified by git before starting (team-01 HEAD, the main checkout HEAD and ls-remote of origin refs/heads/yrsa/feature-roadmap-build all exactly eeff966ab964fee695ac0587d73a2feb107aa7df; team-01 clean). Pure layer only; no push, no merge, the main checkout at mythic-hammer/repo untouched except reading, no README touched, no config / PUBLIC_API / OWNERSHIP / roadmap data / CAMPAIGN_STATE / WAVE_PLAN touched, no other team's worktree touched.
- Roadmap words built to (roadmap_slices.yaml, M-145, Part VI The Crew): "formations can be attached to Part V's scheduled jobs (a dawn Auditor sweep over yesterday's commits)."
- Design, in the M-144 crew_interrupts shape exactly, with the derived-standing rule at its heart where M-144 held the derived-consistency rule: a crew schedule book holds attachment records in held order - one formation attached to one scheduled job (its formation word, the name the M-143 template book summons it by, with its template id; a plain-words title; the detail of what the formation stands on the job to do; and the job held whole as a Part V M-105 ScheduledJob - what it runs as an argv list, never a shell string, when it runs, whether it is enabled, and its approval posture, always naming its job id) - and names NO standing and NO action, because both are DERIVED from the job at call time: an enabled unattended job's formation is armed and runs when its moment comes, an enabled needs-approval job's formation waits for approval at that moment, and a disabled job's formation is owed nothing, so a stored standing / status / action / schedule_text key on a record refuses as an unknown key and no attachment can claim a readiness its job does not give it. One job, one formation: a book or registry holding two attachments on one job id, or two under one attachment id, refuses (one formation may stand on many jobs). A per-attachment report is DERIVED from the record's job - its three checks (formation attached by name, the schedule stated in plain words, the action the standing owes), its standing, its action (cross-checked against its standing and its action check at construction) and its account read off the record - never stored loose: a stored report whose template, job, schedule, checks, standing, action or account disagree with the derived report refuses, and a report naming no account refuses too. The attachment index is DERIVED from the whole book: its rows are the per-attachment reports in held order, its totals are the records' own derived counts (every attachment held, those armed, those waiting, those on disabled jobs, and every distinct formation standing on a job - an index whose totals are not its own rows' counts refuses at construction) and its account is read off the rows and totals; a stored index that disagrees refuses. Validated records carry alias spellings throughout (template/formation, title/headline, detail/description/purpose, job/scheduled_job, attachment_id/id; schedule_text/schedule, standing/status, checks/findings, account/statements; rows/reports and the total_* names with their shorter spellings) with two-spellings-with-different-values refusals and unknown-key refusals on records, reports, indexes, the guide head and the book mapping alike - the M-105 job layer's own alias spellings ride inside the embedded job mapping, read by that layer itself. Registry: CrewScheduleRegistry holds records in held order with deterministic attachment-N auto ids, duplicate attachment-id and duplicate job-id refusals, first book sets the title, reports and the index derived at call time, never held. Records shaped as session news (attachment_record / report_record / index_record / book_record, types crew_schedule_attachment / crew_schedule_report / crew_schedule_index / crew_schedule_book, slice M-145, news + headline). Plain words (plain_words per record, report_plain_words and index_plain_words per report/index or book), formatters (format_attachment / format_attachments / format_report / format_index / format_crew_schedules_screen) and the shared parser parse_crew_schedules_args for the /crewschedules grammar. Public names: 37 in __all__ by live scan on the laptop.
- Composition decisions, grounded in what the shipped modules actually hold (checked on the laptop before composing anything): composed by import ONLY where the other layer truly holds the record vocabulary - core/scheduled_work (M-105) composed whole: ScheduledJob / validate_scheduled_job read an attachment's job by that layer's own builders, format_schedule states its schedule in plain words, and normalize_job_id reads its job id, so every attached job answers to exactly the validator the live scheduled jobs answer to; and core/crew_members.normalize_member_id (M-121) composed for the formation word, the template id and the attachment/report/index ids - the exact reading core/crew_templates (M-143) gives a formation's own word, so a formation is named exactly as the template book names one. Read and NOT composed, with reasons: core/crew_templates.py records themselves (M-143 - an attachment names a formation by its word and template id; it does not restate the formation's seats, which stay the template book's own record, exactly as M-144 read templates without composing them); the M-105 agent execution road (Agent.scheduled_check / _carry_scheduled_run in agents/llm.py - it genuinely carries a due unattended job out as a real M-103 background job and records the run, but nothing in it knows formations, and this slice does not hook it: attaching a formation changes no execution and claims none); core/crew_members.py casting records (M-121 - members stand inside formations, which the template book already holds; an attachment names the formation, never its members); core/background_tasks.py (M-103 - the live managed-job mechanism a due job is carried out through, a mechanism, not a schedule vocabulary); and core/keeper.py (M-104 - it judges whether live work has gone quiet, a liveness verdict, not a joining of a formation to a job).
- Shipped example (mythic_agent/data/crew_schedules.yaml, schema mythic.crew_schedules/1, held to its pinned shape by the loader's example check): the crew schedule book at the M-144 slice boundary - the roadmap's own example first: att-dawn-auditor-sweep (formation review_panel, tpl-review-panel, on job dawn-auditor-sweep - argv git log --since=yesterday --oneline, daily at 06:00, enabled, unattended - armed), att-weekly-full-six (full_six on weekly-full-six, weekly on monday at 09:00, needs approval - waiting_for_approval), att-research-swarm-watch (research_swarm on research-swarm-watch, every 12 hours from its anchor - armed) and att-weekend-duo-paused (duo on weekend-duo-sweep, weekly on saturday at 08:00, the job disabled - the honest counter-example that is owed nothing) - plus one pinned index index-m144-boundary: rows in held order (review_panel, full_six, research_swarm, duo); totals 4 attachments, 2 armed, 1 waiting, 1 disabled, 4 distinct formations, its account and the first (dawn Auditor sweep) report's checks and account pinned word-for-word.
- Name-clash checks, recorded per the task: no crew_schedules module, data file or test existed anywhere in the tree before this slice; the command word crewschedules is free on all surfaces - "crewschedules" and "crew_schedule" appear 0 times in mythic_agent/terminal.py, mythic_agent/agents/command_handler.py, mythic_agent/ui/screens/chat_screen.py and mythic_agent/agents/llm.py (the scheduled-work roads answer to /schedule, scheduled_work_status and _scheduled_registry, none of which this slice's names touch); the module name crew_schedules clashes with nothing across core, the agents/UI surfaces or the existing data files. NO config delta: mythic_agent/data/config_defaults.yaml was searched for schedule / formation / crew settings and none exists to gate (the word schedule appears 0 times in it), exactly as the sibling slices found for their domains, so there is nothing for a setting to gate.
- In-slice defects: two failures while building, both in this slice's own new tests and fixed in-slice - test_registry_auto_ids_and_duplicates first asserted that registering a fresh job/id pair refused (it must not: only duplicate attachment ids and duplicate job ids refuse, and the assertion now names a same-job clash), and asserted a title for a registry that had only one-by-one holdings (such a registry holds no title - its book() raises, exactly as the sibling registries behave - and the assertion now pins the held list instead). The pure module and shipped book were green as written: the shipped example validated whole on its first load, pinned checks, standings, totals and accounts all matching.
- Tests: tests/test_crew_schedules.py, header TIER: integration in the sibling pattern: 25 pure tests covering constants + usage + the standings vocabulary; the shipped example shape (pinned title, ids, formations, jobs and standings in held order, derived actions run/await_approval/run/none, the template / job / standing lookups and their KeyErrors); the shipped index (pinned rows, totals 4/2/1/1/4, derivation matching rows and account, the first report's pinned checks and account); record validation with alias spellings (formation/headline/description/scheduled_job/id); two-spellings refusals; unknown-key refusals incl. the stored standing keys standing / status / action / schedule_text / armed / members / size; the one-job-one-formation rule in a book and in the registry; malformed records (bad formation word, empty title, missing job, a job naming no job id, a shell-string argv refused by the M-105 layer, a word-named schedule time refused by the M-105 layer, an unknown key inside the job mapping); standing derivation across all four enabled/approval combinations; report derivation incl. the waiting and disabled accounts; stored reports judged against their attachment; account-less report refusal; a report whose action or action check contradicts its standing refusing at construction, too-few-checks and wrong-formation-check refusals; index derivation incl. row lookup and the distinct-formation total; stored indexes judged against their records incl. the totals-not-rows'-counts construction refusal; duplicate attachment-id and duplicate index-id refusals in a book; a book index disagreeing with its book refusing (and a wrong schema refusing); file loader round-trip + missing file; registry auto ids (attachment-1, attachment-2) + duplicate-id and duplicate-job refusals + derived report and index + the no-title book refusal; registry book title-clash refusal; session-news records for all four record kinds incl. the disabled headline; plain words for records, reports and the index; formatters + template and standing filters + the whole screen; the parser grammar (show / attachment / template / job / report / index plus bad-grammar refusals); and the builders from field words. Run on the laptop in team-01 with the campaign venv: solo 25 passed. The full suite was NOT run (banking's step, not this layer's); neighbour suites run with the agent layer, below.
- HONEST BOUNDARY, pinned by test: data only - the pure layer starts no timer, runs no job, summons or spawns no crew, calls no model and persists nothing; an attachment is a validated record held as data, and its embedded job record stays the scheduled-work layer's own record (its slice word is M-105). Attaching a formation changes no M-105 execution: the agent layer's scheduled check still carries due jobs out as plain background jobs exactly as before, with no crew involved, and this slice claims no such run. No Part VIII board is claimed; attachments are visible through this slice's own /crewschedules screen.
- Still owed before M-145 can bank: the agent layer (a session CrewScheduleRegistry on Agent in the sibling shape, /crewschedules on both roads sharing parse_crew_schedules_args and the pure formatters, chat_screen help/usage/routing - NO config delta expected, as no schedule or formation setting exists to gate), then the banking itself, in roadmap order (merge into the campaign main checkout -> full suite on the laptop -> PUBLIC_API regen (+37 pure names expected) -> OWNERSHIP regen -> roadmap flip of M-145 to done -> ledger row -> dead-code quarantine only if the banking gate flags one -> closure commit -> explicit-refspec push -> ls-remote verify -> campaign state update). Banking is a separate keeper step and was NOT done here.
